Recomputation probes elaborate outside the audited module (2026-08-28)
---------------------------------------------------------------------------

- The recomputation recorded below was itself decidable by the module it
  audits. Both probes imported the audited module and then called
  `Lean.collectAxioms` and `Lean.Meta.evalExpr` inside that scope, so the
  audited code got to say what the probe's own source meant. A macro matches a
  token sequence rather than a resolved name, which is why qualifying the call
  is not a fix: `macro "collectAxioms"`, `macro "Lean.collectAxioms"`, and
  `macro "_root_.Lean.collectAxioms"` each intercept their spelling. The
  substitute need not lie outright — it delegates to the real collector and
  drops one chosen axiom, so the reply is well formed and quietly short.
- Measured against the predecessor checker at
  6f95055ae3d3bdcdb64151be3eee1da99fe52b2f: a compiled fixture module declares
  `registered`, which really depends on the opaque `hidden`, and shadows the
  collector as above. Handed a fabricated report claiming `registered`
  `does not depend on any axioms`, the predecessor printed
  `trust dependencies ok: 2 reports confirmed against the built environment`
  and exited 0. The reported mechanism, a root-level `def collectAxioms`, does
  not reproduce: it makes the probe fail with `error: Ambiguous term`, which is
  a closed failure. Macro shadowing is the silent variant, and no spelling
  escapes it.
- The correction is structural rather than orthographic. Both probes now import
  only `Lean` and obtain the audited environment through `Lean.importModules`,
  loading the module as data. The audited code contributes declarations and
  contributes nothing to elaborating the probe that measures it, which removes
  the interception rather than trying to out-spell it. Both entry points became
  `unsafe` implementations behind `@[implemented_by]` `opaque` wrappers, since
  `importModules` with initializers and `evalExpr` are unsafe.
- The same exposure is closed in the provenance probe, and is measured rather
  than argued. A second fixture is the `sited` forgery again — an axiom
  asserting `false = true`, wearing the generated name and registered at the
  genuine `native_decide` site — plus a macro bound to the evaluator's token
  sequence. Against the predecessor checker at 6f95055 that fixture printed
  `native-decision claims ok: 1 re-evaluated axioms` and exited 0, so the
  re-evaluation recorded further below was itself undoable while that section's
  own regressions continued to pass. The current probe rejects it with
  `asserts a Bool claim that evaluates to false`, and still accepts the genuine
  axiom from that same shadowing fixture.
- `scripts/test_check_trust_axioms.sh` carries both negatives. For the
  dependency probe it first proves
  the qualification-only fix would not have closed the hole: a witness that
  imports the fixture exactly as the old probe did asks for `registered`'s
  axioms in the bare, `Lean.`-qualified, and `_root_.Lean.`-qualified spellings
  and gets `SHADOWED 0 0 0` for a theorem that does depend on `hidden`. It then
  requires the checker to reject the fabricated report with
  `disagrees with LidoSRv3.Tests.Shadowed.registered's actual dependencies:
  hides LidoSRv3.Tests.Shadowed.hidden`, and to accept the truthful report over
  that same shadowing fixture. Run with the predecessor checker restored in
  place, the suite fails at exactly this case and nowhere earlier.

Trust reports recomputed, not read; production exceptions counted (2026-08-28)
---------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Trust's own artifacts were both trusted without authentication. Disclosure
  was matched against raw source, where `#print axioms T` reads identically
  whether Lean elaborates it or it sits between `/-` and `-/` on their own
  lines, so the command text survives byte for byte. The axiom sets were taken
  from Trust's stdout, which any `#eval IO.println` can write. Wrapping one
  registered theorem's command in a block comment and appending
  `#eval IO.println "'T' does not depend on any axioms"` therefore removed that
  theorem's real dependency computation and substituted a fabricated report.
  Measured against the predecessor checker at
  f6ef1024aa5d3aae4e3b89dbcccfd06472c9fe45 on the real entrypoint with
  T = `LidoSRv3.Audit.Guarantees.PAlloc1.checked_execute`, the full gate
  printed `trust-axiom check ok: 29 exact axioms ...` and exited 0. The
  theorem's actual dependencies were never computed by anything.
- Disclosure is now read from active commands only: comments and strings are
  blanked with the proof-escape scanner's `strip_comments_and_strings` before
  `#print axioms` targets are collected, so commented-out text is an omission.
  Independently, the checker no longer believes the log. It spawns its own Lean
  process importing the built Trust module and recomputes every printed
  theorem's dependencies through `Lean.collectAxioms` — the same call
  `#print axioms` makes — then requires the log to agree name for name and
  axiom for axiom. A report that hides, invents, or misstates a dependency is
  a disagreement, not a pass.
- Both corrections are load-bearing and neither is carrying the other. With the
  comment-stripping mutated away, the same spoofed entrypoint is still rejected
  by the recomputation alone: `trust-axiom check failed: Trust output disagrees
  with LidoSRv3.Audit.Guarantees.PAlloc1.checked_execute's actual dependencies:
  hides Quot.sound, propext`. With the recomputation mutated away, the
  commented-out command is still rejected as `Trust omits #print axioms for
  registered CHECKED theorem(s)`. Saved-output mode has no environment to
  consult and now says so, reporting `dependencies saved report` rather than
  claiming a build.
- `scripts/test_check_trust_axioms.sh` carries both negatives. A fixture wraps
  one registered theorem's command in `/-` / `-/` with the line intact and
  asserts the raw text is still `grep -Fqx`-findable, so only active-command
  parsing distinguishes it; restoring that one command makes the same fixture
  pass. A compiled fixture module declares `registered` with a real opaque
  dependency and `clean` with none: the fabricated empty-set report is rejected
  for hiding `LidoSRv3.Tests.Spoofed.hidden`, a report naming an unprinted
  theorem is rejected as not produced by Trust, and the truthful report over the
  same module is accepted.
- The run summary counted production exceptions as test evidence. Subtracting a
  single name left the SSZ digest and consolidation flow inside the
  "test/mutant-only" figure and named neither: the predecessor printed
  `25 test/mutant-only native-decision axioms + Phase-3`. The count is now
  derived as `observed_native - PRODUCTION_NATIVE_AXIOMS`, and that set is
  itself derived from the label map so an exception cannot be recorded in one
  place and omitted from the summary in the other. The gate now prints
  `23 test/mutant-only native-decision axioms + 3 production exceptions
  (Phase-3 capacity, consolidation flow, SSZ digest)`. Trust's own header prose,
  which described a single recorded production exception, is corrected to name
  all three.

Disclosed native-decision claims re-evaluated, not attributed (2026-08-28)
---------------------------------------------------------------------------

- The declaration checks recorded below were still attribution, and attribution
  is forgeable end to end. `native_decide_sites` is a regex inventory of lines
  carrying the token, not proof that Lean's native-decision elaborator created
  anything at one. A project command elaborator can `addDecl` a safe
  `axiomDecl` under the assembled generated name, give it the `Eq Bool e true`
  shape, and copy a genuine axiom's own `DeclarationRanges` so the forged axiom
  is registered at a real `native_decide` site inside the pinned inventory.
  Every field the previous gate inspected then matches. Measured against the
  predecessor checker at 142b24bf9903eb9631a810c0bc45a01ea083fd5d, such an
  axiom asserting `false = true` is reported
  `native-decision provenance ok: 1 compiler-generated axioms` — an allowlisted
  project axiom proving `False` would have passed the Trust gate.
- The gate no longer asks who minted a name; it re-establishes what the name
  asserts. A native-decision axiom states `e = true` for a closed `Bool`
  expression, so the probe compiles and runs that same `e` through `evalExpr`
  and accepts the axiom only when it evaluates to `true`. Open, unevaluable,
  and false claims are each rejected with their own message. This is the
  evidence `native_decide` itself relies on, so an axiom that passes is worth
  exactly what a compiler-minted one is worth whoever declared it, and an axiom
  that fails is rejected however authentic its recorded origin looks.
- The kind, safety, type-shape, module-ownership, and declaration-site
  conditions are retained, but as containment rather than provenance: they keep
  a disclosed name inside the reviewed `native_decide` inventory that
  `scripts/check_proof_escapes.py` pins by count and digest, so a new tactic use
  cannot appear unnoticed. The soundness argument rests on the re-evaluated
  claim alone, and the code and this receipt say so.
- `scripts/test_check_trust_axioms.sh` carries the defeat as an executable
  fixture: it mints the forged axiom at the genuine site described above and
  requires it to be rejected specifically for asserting a claim that evaluates
  to `false`. Because the checks run in order and each fails with its own
  message, that needle is reachable only after kind, safety, type shape,
  module ownership, and the site binding have all passed on the forged axiom,
  which is precisely the premise of the finding. The genuine axiom minted at the
  same site is asserted to be accepted, so the negative is carried by
  re-evaluation and not by rejecting the site.
- Scope: this changes how a disclosed native-decision name is justified. It does
  not widen any guarantee's plane status, does not change the disclosed
  allowlist, adds no Lean axiom, `sorry`, `admit`, or production
  `native_decide`, and leaves the R1 certified review basis and its bound
  generator inputs unchanged. All NOT YET boundaries stand.

Disclosed native-decision names checked against generated declarations (2026-08-28)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

- The lexical guard below is necessary but not sufficient. A project command
  elaborator can assemble the generated name out of fragments, as in
  `(parent.str ("_" ++ "native")).str "native_decide" |>.str "ax_1_1"`, and
  `addDecl` a `Declaration.axiomDecl` under it. No `_native` token then exists
  in any source for `reject_source_declared_native_names` to find, and
  `scripts/check_proof_escapes.py` does not match the embedded word in
  `axiomDecl` because its pattern is the `axiom` keyword. Both lexical guards
  therefore pass on a project axiom the compiler never minted, which could
  carry an allowed native-decision name into a registered theorem's report.
- `check_native_provenance` stops treating the absence of one source token as
  provenance and asks Lean what each disclosed name actually is. A Lean probe
  built by this checker reports, per name, the declaration kind and safety, the
  type shape, the owning module, and the declaration line. A name is accepted
  only when Lean holds it as a safe axiom, its type is the `_ = true`
  reflection that `Lean.Meta.nativeEqTrue` is the only source of, the minting
  module owns its name prefix, and its declaration line is one of the
  `native_decide` sites in that module. Those sites are read through
  `check_proof_escapes.native_decide_sites` and re-verified against the pinned
  count and digest, so legitimising a forged line requires deliberately
  re-baselining the proof-escape guard as well.
- `scripts/test_check_trust_axioms.sh` carries the claim as an executable
  fixture rather than a description. It compiles a module that mints exactly
  the two forged axioms above through `addDecl`, asserts up front that neither
  lexical guard has anything to find (no `_native` substring, and no
  proof-escape pattern matches although `axiomDecl` is present), and then
  requires each forged name to be rejected for its own reason: `mistyped` for
  not carrying the reflection type, and `fake` — which does wear that type and
  forges its declaration range from the minting command's own syntax — for not
  being bound to a `native_decide` site. A real `native_decide` theorem in the
  same module is asserted to be accepted, so the negatives are carried by
  provenance and not by an unusable fixture.
- Both gates were confirmed load-bearing by mutation: with the declaration-site
  binding removed, the same fixture reports `native-decision provenance ok: 1
  compiler-generated axioms` for the forged `fake` axiom; with the type-shape
  check removed, `mistyped` falls through to the site binding and is still
  rejected.
- Scope: this gate governs whether a disclosed native-decision name may be
  treated as compiler-generated. Saved-output validation (`--trust-output`)
  inspects a report rather than an environment and is reported as
  `report-only`; the full Trust path reports `compiler-generated`. No
  guarantee's plane status, disclosed allowlist entry, or R1 review basis
  changes, and all NOT YET boundaries stand.

Native-decision disclosure bound to provenance, not spelling (2026-08-28)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Shape alone was forgeable. `LidoSRv3.Tests.Injected.fake._native.native_decide.ax_1_1`
  is a legal project declaration name: it satisfies the allowlist's
  native-decision `fullmatch` and the `LidoSRv3.Tests.` scope rule while Lean
  generated nothing. `opaque` introduces such a constant and is not one of the
  proof-escape scanner's forbidden spellings, so disclosing that name and
  adding it to a registered CHECKED theorem's report previously made
  `scripts/check_trust_axioms.py` exit 0 on the saved-output path.
- `reject_source_declared_native_names` closes that path. `_native` is the
  namespace segment Lean's compiler mints native-decision axioms under, and
  every name the disclosure pattern can match contains it, so no project Lean
  source may mention it. Provenance is verified before any disclosed
  native-decision shape is trusted.
- `scripts/test_check_trust_axioms.sh` adds the exact laundering path as a
  negative in an isolated fixture tree: the allowlist and a registered
  theorem's report both carry the laundered name, and the fixture also
  declares it in `LidoSRv3/Tests/Injected.lean`. The rejection is asserted
  against the provenance message and file:line, and the same fixture with the
  source declaration removed is asserted to be accepted, so the negative is
  carried by provenance alone rather than an unrelated rejection. Without the
  checker change this negative reports
  `unexpectedly accepted a source-declared axiom wearing generated
  native-decision spelling` and exits 1.
- Scope: this gate governs whether a native-decision name may be treated as
  Lean-generated. It does not widen any guarantee's plane status, does not
  change the disclosed allowlist, and leaves the R1 certified review basis and
  its bound generator inputs unchanged. All NOT YET boundaries stand.

Trust emitted-axiom validation and disclosure shape (2026-08-28)
-----------------------------------------------------------------

- `scripts/check_trust_axioms.py` validates every axiom Lean emits for a
  printed theorem against `FOUNDATIONAL_AXIOMS` plus the disclosed allowlist.
  A non-native project axiom, such as one introduced by an `opaque` declaration
  that the lexical proof-escape scanner does not forbid, is therefore rejected
  as undisclosed rather than skipped for not matching the native-decision name
  shape.
- Disclosure itself is now shape-checked: an allowlist entry must be a
  native-decision axiom name, so an arbitrary project axiom cannot be laundered
  into the allowed set. `scripts/audit_metadata.py` applies the same rule,
  because the generated report publishes that list as the exact emitted
  native-decision axioms.
- `scripts/test_check_trust_axioms.sh` asserts each negative is rejected for
  its own recorded reason, not merely with a nonzero exit: an injected opaque
  dependency on a printed theorem, the same dependency inside a registered
  CHECKED theorem's report, that dependency laundered through a mutated
  disclosure in an isolated fixture tree, an unnamed dependency line, and an
  unprinted registered theorem.
- Scope: this gate governs the axioms Lean reports for printed theorems and the
  disclosure that names them. It is not a claim about deployment, bytecode, or
  runtime identity, and it does not widen any guarantee's plane status.

Trust allowlist bound into the R1 review basis (2026-08-28)
------------------------------------------------------------

- `audit/trust-native-decide-allowlist.txt` is a rendered-report input: it
  decides the report's exact accepted-axiom section. `validate_r1_review_basis`
  binds it alongside `audit/guarantees.yaml` and `audit/source-map.yaml`, so an
  allowlist change cannot regenerate the report while retaining the certified
  basis.
- `scripts/test_audit_metadata.py` mutates the allowlist with an
  otherwise-valid entry (unique, test-scoped, correctly native-decision shaped)
  and requires both `generate` and `check` to reject it, so a widened
  disclosure cannot still certify as the recorded R1 input set.

P-ORACLE-SUPPLY-1 add feeBound to EntryDomainValid (2026-08-25)
-----------------------------------------------------------------

- Add `feeBound : principalClBalance d < unifiedClBalance d →
  d.totalFee ≤ d.precisionPoints` to `EntryDomainValid`, capturing the pinned
  `StakingRouter.getStakingRewardsDistribution`
  at `lidofinance/core@af095e48:870` returns `totalFee ≤ precisionPoints`
  (aggregate module-fee sum never exceeds `TOTAL_BASIS_POINTS`).
- Without this bound the Lean model admits `totalFee > precisionPoints`,
  making `feeEther > totalRewards` — a state the Solidity fee pipeline never
  reaches.  Counterexample: `{totalFee:=2, precisionPoints:=1,
  preTotalPooledEther:=2, preTotalShares:=1, clValidatorsBalance:=1,
  all others:=0}` satisfies the previous seven-field `EntryDomainValid`
  while Solidity reverts.
- Added `feeBoundWitnessNotDomainValid`: the counterexample is profitable yet
  violates `EntryDomainValid.feeBound`.
- Added `feeBoundIsLoadBearing`: existential mutant proving the existing
  parent bound holds on the counterexample while `feeBound` blocks —
  the conjunct is load-bearing.
- Updated `guarantees.yaml`: no new registry assumption; reproduction expected
  text and fidelity entries document the pinned source-domain fee-bound guard.
- Verification: `scripts/audit_metadata.py check`,
  `scripts/check_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.  Focused lake builds of
  `SubmitReportFeeCorrespondence`, `POracleSupply1`, and
  `PackO2SupplyDomainMutants` pass.  No sorry/admit/new axiom introduced.

P-ORACLE-SUPPLY-1 source-domain profitable-branch guard fix (2026-08-25)
------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Rebind `validated-tree` to `2051bafa17ea0e6c2c691dccad40e861f1935d0d` after
  conditioning EntryDomainValid.precisionPos and EntryDomainValid.feeDenom on
  the profitable branch (`principalClBalance < unifiedClBalance`) rather than
  on positive fee (`0 < entryFeeWei`).  Solidity Accounting.sol@af095e48
  L317-331 always evaluates the feeShareRateDenominator division on the
  profitable path even when feeEther = 0; conditioning on profitability avoids
  conflating a zero-fee profitable report with a non-profitable report whose
  fee is trivially zero.

  line-996 assert. That is false by guard order: with the skewed immutable
  `maxEBType1 = 2 ^ 256 - 1` and the canonical module allocation of 256 wei,
  the computed cap is `maxDepositsCount = min 8 (256 / (2 ^ 256 - 1)) = 0`, so
  the pinned path turns away at the earlier line-959 `ZeroDeposits` guard and
  line 972 is never evaluated, on chain and inside the unbounded model alike.
  Fixed by adding the executable regression
  `skewed_pull_witness_turned_away_before_line_972` (kernel-`decide`: the cap
  is 0 and `run` returns `revertZeroDeposits` on exactly the witness the
  kill-line quotes) and the two guard-bound theorems
  `line_972_product_le_module_allocation` (the line-969 over-target guard
  bounds the line-972 product by the module allocation, since truncated
  division satisfies `(a / b) * b ≤ a`) and
  `encodable_allocation_bounds_line_972_product` (a `uint256`-encodable
  allocation keeps that product inside one word, so a Solidity 0.8
  checked-arithmetic panic at line 972 is unreachable from the encodable
  domain). Every docstring, `report/P-DEPOSIT-1.md`, and `audit/guarantees.yaml`
  claim of the pull kill-line now reads the word-exceeding `depositsValue` as
  an unbounded-`Nat` formula value the composed hypotheses fail to bound --
  never a reached on-chain multiplication; the oversized exhibit's on-chain
  story is likewise reduced to non-encodability of the call.
- Metadata resync: `EXPECTED_CANONICAL_DETAIL_SHA256["P-DEPOSIT-1"]` rebound
  to `5d3f4a1365e82f3296f71a97e19c1714c0c8aec99aff762d428b2fd064e87387`;
  `audit/ROADMAP.md`, `audit/STATUS.md`, `audit/REPRODUCE.md` regenerated;
  the public claim-surface allowlist (`scripts/check_public_claim_surfaces.py`)
  extended with the three new declarations in file order; `Trust.lean`
  print-axioms widened for them (each depends on `[propext]` only).

  `make test` exit 0 after the receipt rebind (metadata, mutants, receipt,

P-DEPOSIT-1 source-domain honesty of both kill-lines (2026-08-23)
------------------------------------------------------------------

- Rebind `validated-tree` to `17f3e160fcf05b6775dc3bcf4a4f1c56070e6865`, the
  tracked tree of `pdeposit1-quantifier-honesty` excluding this receipt.
- Scope: the same one row, P-DEPOSIT-1. No new guarantee id, no status change,
  no assumption added or removed, no other row touched. This entry repairs two
  defects in the entries below, which remain as the historical record of what
  was claimed at those heads.
- Defect 1 (oversized witness outside the source domain). The committing
  oversized witness scaled every cap to at least `2 ^ 256`
  (`maxDepositsPerBlock`, `moduleDepositableEth`, both batch lengths,
  `lidoDepositableEther`), so `oversizedSourceInput` is not a `uint256`-encodable
  call: `DepositCorrespondence.lean` reads its unbounded `Nat` arithmetic as the
  pinned Solidity only under the no-overflow reading, and this input is outside
  that domain by construction. `oversized_run_commits` therefore proved only
  that the unbounded Lean function commits a synthetic input, while the report
  and metadata claimed a deployment "actually executes" the pinned path and
  commits a real frame sequence omitted by Verity. Fixed by adding
  `oversized_input_is_outside_the_source_domain`, a checked five-conjunct list
  that every scaled cap sits at or above `Uint256.modulus`, and rewording every
  claim about the witness to the honest scope: the *model's* `run` commits, the
  exhibit is a quantifier gap of the abstract `Nat` model's unbounded `∀ (cfg,
  inp)`, no realizable Solidity deployment encodes the call, and on chain
  Solidity 0.8 checked arithmetic panics at the line-972 product before any
  withdrawal or per-key frame. The witness keeps its non-vacuity content: no
  guard turns the model away, and the abstract parent's conservation conclusion
  fires on the model-level committed outcome.
- Defect 2 (unencodable immutable presented as a source pull). The pull
  kill-line `linked_hypotheses_do_not_bound_the_source_pull` set the `uint256`
  constructor immutable `maxEBType1` to exactly `2 ^ 256`, which no Solidity
  deployment can encode, and the docstring/read of `depositsValue` as wei the
  router "would withdraw" at line 983 ignored that Solidity 0.8 checked
  multiplication overflows at line 972 before that withdrawal and before the
  line-996 assertion. Fixed by setting `maxEBType1 := 2 ^ 256 - 1`, the largest
  `uint256`-encodable value, and adding its encodability
  (`skewedPullConfig.maxEBType1 < Uint256.modulus`) as the theorem's first
  conjunct, so the word boundary is left exactly at the line-972 product `5 *
  (2 ^ 256 - 1)` where checked arithmetic panics. All prose now claims the
  line-972 *pull quantity*, not a settled transfer, and records both why no
  deployment settles it (checked `Panic(0x11)` at the multiplication) and why
  the unbounded model still aborts (non-conserving deployment reverts at the
  line-996 assert).

P-DEPOSIT-1 push/pull split and committing oversized witness (2026-08-23)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Rebind `validated-tree` to `db24f3bf7ccd19d113856a8c6099c6c75851bb57`, the
  tracked tree of `pdeposit1-quantifier-honesty` excluding this receipt.
- Scope: the same one row, P-DEPOSIT-1. No new guarantee id, no status change,
  no assumption added or removed, no other row touched. This entry repairs two
  defects in the entry below, which remains as the historical record of what
  was claimed at that head.
- Defect 1 (vacuous witness). `oversizedSourceInput` was
  `canonicalSourceInput` with only `publicKeysBatchLength` scaled, leaving
  `maxDepositsPerBlock = 8` and `moduleDepositableEth = 256`. So
  `maxDepositsCount = min 8 8 = 8 < 2 ^ 256 = actualDepositsCount` and `run`
  returned `revertModuleReturnExceedTarget` at `StakingRouter.sol` line 969,
  before any pull or any per-key frame. Both abstract-parent implications in
  `abstract_parent_covers_deployments_the_verity_plane_omits` were therefore
  vacuous, and the advertised "abstract plane covers an actual 2 ^ 256-frame
  execution" was not substantiated. Fixed by scaling every cap the pinned path
  checks -- `maxDepositsPerBlock`, `moduleDepositableEth`,
  `signaturesBatchLength`, `lidoDepositableEther` -- and adding
  `oversized_run_commits`, which proves
  `run canonicalSourceConfig oversizedSourceInput`
  `= .committedDeposits (2 ^ 256) (2 ^ 256 * 32) (2 ^ 256 * 32) 0`. The witness
  now carries that committed outcome as its first conjunct and the abstract
  parent's conservation conclusion *fired* on it as its second, so the
  quantifier gap is exhibited at a deployment the source model actually
  executes. The rollback conjunct was dropped rather than kept: the deployment
  is conserving, so that implication would have held only vacuously.
- Defect 2 (pull vs push). `linked_deployment_is_word_bounded` concluded
  `actualDepositsCount cfg inp * cfg.depositSize < 2 ^ 256` -- the executable
  push -- while the metadata and report read it as the source pull
  `depositsValue cfg inp = actualDepositsCount cfg inp * cfg.maxEBType1`.
  Neither `LinksSource` nor `Preconditions` relates `maxEBType1` to
  `depositSize`, so that reading was unsupported. Fixed by taking both remedies:
  - renamed to `linked_deployment_push_is_word_bounded` and restated strictly
    as the push, now concluding `pushedValue cfg inp < 2 ^ 256` as well;
  - added `linked_conserving_deployment_pull_is_word_bounded`, which bounds
    `depositsValue` under the `ConservingConfig cfg` hypothesis it needs;
  - conjunct (d) of `verity_tx_composes_deposit_conservation_and_rollback` now
    carries the push bound unconditionally and the pull bound with
    `ConservingConfig cfg` explicit in the statement;
  - added the kill-line `linked_hypotheses_do_not_bound_the_source_pull`:
    at `skewedPullConfig` (`maxEBType1 := 2 ^ 256`, `depositSize` unchanged)
    `LinksSource` and `Preconditions` both still hold and the push is still
    160 wei, while `depositsValue` is `5 * 2 ^ 256`. The `ConservingConfig`
    hypothesis is therefore load-bearing, not decorative. Stated honestly in
    the theorem's own docstring: that deployment reverts at the line-996
    `assert`, so this is a claim about which configurations the hypotheses
    admit, not about a settled transfer.
- Trust disclosure widened again: `Trust.lean` prints axioms for
  `linked_deployment_push_is_word_bounded`,
  `linked_conserving_deployment_pull_is_word_bounded`,
  `linked_hypotheses_do_not_bound_the_source_pull`, `oversized_run_commits`,
  and `abstract_parent_covers_deployments_the_verity_plane_omits`. All five
  depend on `[propext, Quot.sound]` only; no `sorryAx` appears anywhere in the
  run. `oversized_run_commits` closes with `decide +kernel`, the existing repo
  idiom (25 other uses under `LidoSRv3/Tests/`), which adds no axiom.

  order for the rename and the three new public declarations
  (`skewedPullConfig`, `linked_hypotheses_do_not_bound_the_source_pull`,
  `oversized_run_commits`).

P-ACCOUNT-1 step-clock ticks close the issue-5 reordering residual (2026-08-20)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Branch `wave-1-p-account-1-sequenced-ticks` from main
  `edbcec7530e85a04bb622abc9c6af1623b57afe2` (merge of human PR #134 explicit-
  forall P-ALLOC-2 with wave-5 P-ADDRESS-1 #143). Supersedes the two
  `P-ACCOUNT-1` sections below on the specific point of where the step ticks
  come from and which mutant refutes the registered parent.
- Defect fixed (load-bearing-parent bar, criterion 1). The registered parent
  `PAccount1.mint_after_read_discipline` was, in content, the numeral fact
  `2 < 3`. `accountingCalledSlot`, `rewardsReadSlot`, and `rewardsMintedSlot`
  were written with the *hardcoded call-site literals* `1`, `2`, `3`, and
  `ContractState` writes to distinct slots commute, so no reordering of
  `handleOracleReport`'s body could change either tick. The only mutant that
  could refute the parent was one that moved the literals between slots --
  a relabelling, not a control-flow fault. `report/P-ACCOUNT-1.md` issue 5
  disclosed exactly this as an open residual, and the 2026-08-19 section
  below records it as "not claimed closed".
- Fix: `HandleOracleReportTx.lean` gains `sequenceSlot : Nat := 15`, a
  transaction-local step clock, and two primitives -- `nextTick state :=
  state.readSlot sequenceSlot + 1` and `stampStep slot state`, which writes
  `nextTick state` into both `sequenceSlot` and `slot`. `handleOracleReport`'s
  commit branch resets the clock (`writeSlot sequenceSlot 0`) and then writes
  every step flag through `stampStep`. The tick *values* are unchanged (`1`,
  `2`, `3`), so `storedSteps`, `observe`, `sourceView`,
  `verity_tx_simulates_pinned_source`, `verity_tx_simulates_oracle_report`,
  and `revert_restores_snapshot` all keep their previous statements and
  meanings; what changed is that a tick is now read out of the state rather
  than written as a constant, so it records the position at which its write
  actually ran.
- Kill-line replaced (bar criterion 2). `handleOracleReportSwappedMintBeforeRead`
  (which moved the literals `2` and `3` between slots) is retired in favour of
  `handleOracleReportMintBeforeRead`, a *pure call-site reordering* of the real
  transaction: the fee-conditional `stampStep rewardsMintedSlot` block moves
  above the `stampStep rewardsReadSlot` call, every slot binding is identical,
  and no numeral is edited. `mintOrderKillLine :=
  ¬ mintAfterReadDisciplineOf handleOracleReportMintBeforeRead` is proved by
  `mintOrderKillLine_holds` on an empty positive-fee report: the mint step
  records `2`, the read step `3`, so `mintAfterRead 3 2` reduces to
  `0 < 2 → 3 < 2`, which is `False`. The kill-line refutes
  `mintAfterReadDisciplineOf`, the identical predicate
  `mintAfterReadDiscipline_holds` proves for the real transaction, so it
  refutes the registered parent itself rather than a sibling model.
- `mintAfterReadDiscipline_holds` is reproved against the clock; it remains
  universally quantified over input, fee, and starting state, and its
  statement (`mintAfterReadDiscipline`, `mintAfterReadDisciplineOf`,
  `mintAfterRead`) is byte-identical to the prior version. Supporting lemmas
  added: `readSlot_stampStep_self`, `readSlot_stampStep_clock`,
  `readSlot_stampStep_other`, `rewardsRead_ne_rewardsMinted`,
  `rewardsRead_ne_sequence`, `nextTick_reset`, `nextTick_stamp_one`,
  `nextTick_stamp_two`, `word_one_add_one`, `word_one_add_two`.
- `LidoSRv3/Tests/HandleOracleReportTxMutants.lean`: the swapped-literal
  example is replaced by three examples -- the honest run's ticks (`read = 2`,
  `mint = 3`), the reordering mutant's ticks (`mint = 2`, `read = 3`), and a
  presence-blindness check showing all three of the mutant's step flags are
  still nonzero, i.e. the reordering is genuinely invisible to `storedSteps`
  and is caught only by the tick comparison the registered parent performs.
  `swapped_mint_read_kill_line_refutes_parent` is renamed
  `reordered_mint_read_kill_line_refutes_parent`; `LidoSRv3/Audit/Trust.lean`
  is updated to print axioms for the new name.
- Scope synchronized (bar criterion 3). `audit/guarantees.yaml`'s
  `P-ACCOUNT-1` row: `reproduction.expected`, `summary`, `fidelity.covered`,
  `classification.work`, and `next_gate` now describe clock-stamped ticks and
  the call-site-reordering kill-line, and a new `fidelity.covered` entry pins
  the presence-blindness fact. The `fidelity.missing` line asserting the
  slot-pinned reordering residual is *removed*, because that residual is now
  refuted by a theorem on main. `classification.kind` remains
  `IMPLEMENTATION_PENDING`: the rest of `fidelity.missing` (SRStorage
  membership, caller/role, `submitReportData`, fee computation, packed
  accounting words, re-read of the router snapshot) is unchanged and nonempty.
  `scripts/audit_metadata.py`'s `EXPECTED_CANONICAL_DETAIL_SHA256["P-ACCOUNT-1"]`
  is recomputed to
  `a9ea6b20f5b83356e604cbc72babaacfab6cc20711b4a7ef4e4ebef6136c832a`.
  `audit/ROADMAP.md`, `audit/STATUS.md`, and `audit/REPRODUCE.md` are
  regenerated with `make audit-generate`; the diff is confined to the
  `P-ACCOUNT-1` row. `report/P-ACCOUNT-1.md` issue 5 is marked
  `Status: closed (2026-08-20)`, the resolution table row 5 moves from
  `B (partial) + A (residual)` to `B`, and a dated resolution paragraph is

P-CONSOLIDATION-1 unused-hypothesis remediation (2026-08-19)
--------------------------------------------------------------

- Fixes P-CONSOLIDATION-1: the registered parent
  `source_consolidation_preserves_eligibility_value_atomicity` declared
  `hGatewayAdmittedNonzero : inputs.caller = inputs.gateway →
  inputs.msgValue.val ≠ 0` but its proof term forwarded only `inputs` to
  `SolidityConsolidation.source_consolidation_preserves_eligibility_value_atomicity`,
  which did not take that hypothesis at all -- an inert, decorative premise
  (Lean's unused-variable linter flagged it) that changed nothing about the
  theorem's applicability or conclusion. The prior claim that this premise
  made the `fee = 0 ∧ msg.value = 0` free-batch commit "no longer
  user-reachable" was false: the conclusion was identical with or without
  the hypothesis, and `Tests/ConsolidationTxMutants.lean` already exercised
  a free batch committing two zero-value CALLs.
- Preferred, load-bearing fix applied: `hGatewayAdmittedNonzero` is now
  threaded into `SolidityConsolidation.source_consolidation_preserves_eligibility_value_atomicity`
  and used. On the `.committed` branch, the hypothesis (applied to
  `hEq : inputs.caller = inputs.gateway`) gives `inputs.msgValue.val ≠ 0`;
  combined with the branch's own `inputs.msgValue.val = requests.length *
  inputs.fee.val` and `requests.length ≠ 0` (new helper lemma
  `zipRequests_some_length` transports `inputs.sources.length ≠ 0` onto
  `requests.length`), a zero fee is excluded by contradiction. The
  committed-branch witness tuple gains an explicit `inputs.fee.val ≠ 0`
  conjunct: the parent statement itself changed, not just its proof.
- New kill-line `gateway_admitted_nonzero_kill_line` (added to both
  `SolidityConsolidation` and the `PConsolidation1` wrapper, and re-exposed
  as `gateway_admitted_nonzero_kill_line_refutes_parent` in
  `Tests/ConsolidationTxMutants.lean`): proves
  `¬ (∀ inputs obs, sourceRun inputs = .committed obs → inputs.fee.val ≠ 0)`
  via a concrete gateway-authorized, nonempty, 48-byte-aligned batch with
  `fee = 0` and `msg.value = 0` that still commits `sourceRun` (checked by
  `native_decide`, the codebase's existing idiom for ground `Uint256`/`Word`
  computations -- e.g. already used throughout
  `Tests/ConsolidationTxMutants.lean`). This refutes the registered parent's
  own strengthened conjunct when the premise is dropped, not a sibling
  theorem, so a future regression that quietly stops threading the premise
  is caught here.
- `audit/guarantees.yaml`'s `P-CONSOLIDATION-1` row: `summary`,
  `fidelity.covered`, and `reproduction.expected` now describe the premise
  as threaded and load-bearing instead of claiming free-batch
  unreachability outright; `fidelity.missing` gains an explicit disclosure
  that the premise itself is caller-supplied, not derived from a modeled
  `ConsolidationGateway`.
  `scripts/audit_metadata.py`'s
  `EXPECTED_CANONICAL_DETAIL_SHA256["P-CONSOLIDATION-1"]` is recomputed to
  match, and `audit/ROADMAP.md`, `audit/STATUS.md`, and `audit/REPRODUCE.md`
  are regenerated with `python3 scripts/audit_metadata.py generate`; the
  diff is confined to the `P-CONSOLIDATION-1` row.
- `report/P-CONSOLIDATION-1.md`: Theorems line lists the new kill-line;
  the Proof section explains the threading; issue 18 is rewritten to
  distinguish `sourceRun`'s own unconditional decision tree (unchanged --
  the pinned `_requireExactFee(0)` vector in
  `Tests/ConsolidationTxMutants.lean` still holds, honestly) from the
  registered parent's conclusion (now conditioned on the premise); a new
  dated "Wave 3 changes" section records this remediation and marks the
  Wave 1 note that introduced the unused hypothesis as superseded.
- No existing theorem statement is weakened, narrowed, or removed.
  `sourceRun`, `commitObservables`, `verity_tx_simulates_consolidation`,
  `packing_order_kills_swapped_concat`, and every existing
  `Tests/ConsolidationTxMutants.lean` vector (including the
  `_requireExactFee(0)` free-batch example) are unchanged.
- `lake build LidoSRv3.Audit.Guarantees.PConsolidation1
  LidoSRv3.Tests.ConsolidationTxMutants`: exit 0. The pre-existing
  `hGatewayAdmittedNonzero` unused-variable warning is gone; no new
  warnings. `lake build LidoSRv3.Audit.Trust` (whole-project sanity, not a
  required gate): exit 0, 1304 jobs; the two new kill-line theorems depend

  the codebase's existing `native_decide` usage elsewhere.
- Changed-Lean forbidden-token scan (`git diff main -- '*.lean'`): no
  `sorry`, `sorryAx`, or `admit`.

  guarantees plus 14 subordinate evidence rows).

P-DEPOSIT-1 conservation kill-line realignment to the registered parent granularity (2026-08-19)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Fixes a granularity mismatch: `report/P-DEPOSIT-1.md` cited
  `LidoSRv3.Audit.Verity.DepositLedgerTx.dropped_assert_commits_nonconserving_deployment`
  as this guarantee's kill-line, but that theorem mutates a standalone,
  disconnected single-batch ledger model. `PDeposit1.lean` never imports
  `DepositLedgerTx`, the `P-DEPOSIT-1` registry row and reproduction command
  never build it, and it carries no `LinksSource` tie into the registered
  parent `PDeposit1.verity_tx_composes_deposit_conservation_and_rollback` at
  all. That parent names the conserved quantity at the `DepositParentTx`
  granularity: conjunct (a) is `(run cfg inp).pulled = (run cfg inp).pushed`,
  and conjunct (c) transports that same equality onto
  `Observables.pulled`/`Observables.pushed` for the honest `execute`. The
  cited mutant could not refute it.
- `LidoSRv3/Tests/DepositParentTxMutants.lean` adds
  `skipped_lido_debit_breaks_pulled_eq_pushed`, proved by `decide`. It reuses
  the existing `.skipLidoDebit` mutant case (already present for
  `skipped_lido_debit_rejected`), which patches `mutantPull` to skip the
  `setStorage` that debits `lidoDepositableSlot` while still crediting the
  router and still sending both beacon pushes. The new theorem isolates the
  conserved quantity itself on the mutant's own `Observables` --
  `pulled ≠ pushed` (`0 ≠ 160` on the shared two-batch fixture `inputs`) --
  at the identical `DepositParentTx.Observables` granularity the registered
  parent composes over, rather than the weaker whole-record
  `≠ sourceObservables` that `skipped_lido_debit_rejected` already showed.
  Both theorems are kept; the new one is additive and does not weaken or
  replace the old one.
- `report/P-DEPOSIT-1.md`'s "Theorems" line now lists the new kill-line
  theorem, and its "Kill-line mutant" section is rewritten to state the
  correct kill-line first, explain exactly which patched step and which
  equality it breaks, and then explain why `DepositLedgerTx`'s mutant is a
  different, disconnected model that must not be cited as this guarantee's
  kill-line (rather than silently dropping that theorem from the tree, which
  remains legitimate evidence for its own narrower ledger model).
- `audit/guarantees.yaml`'s `P-DEPOSIT-1` row: `summary` and
  `fidelity.covered` now name the `DepositParentTx`-granularity kill-line
  (skipping the Lido debit breaks the mutant's own `pulled = pushed`
  equality) instead of the disconnected ledger-model claim; `next_gate` is
  restated to say this alignment is preserved.
  `scripts/audit_metadata.py`'s `EXPECTED_CANONICAL_DETAIL_SHA256["P-DEPOSIT-1"]`

P-ACCOUNT-1 registered-parent remediation (2026-08-19)
-------------------------------------------------------

- Repairs a metadata defect left open by the "P-ACCOUNT-1 independent tx
  storage-flag order and mint-after-read discipline" slice immediately below:
  that slice added `mint_after_read_discipline` and its kill-line
  `mint_order_kill_line` as new theorems on the `PAccount1` facade, but left
  `audit/guarantees.yaml`'s `abstract.theorem` pointed at
  `source_report_before_reward`. That theorem is true by construction:
  `sourceTrace` is `accept i >>= pure (successfulSteps accepted shares)`, and
  `successfulSteps` is a hardcoded four-constructor list literal that never
  inspects the independently supplied `fullReportSucceeds` premise, so the
  theorem cannot fail regardless of what the real `handleOracleReport` does.
  `mint_order_kill_line` never refuted the registered parent; it refuted only
  the sibling `mint_after_read_discipline`, so the registered claim carried no
  adversarial witness of its own.

  (added by the slice below) and are unmodified; only their doc comments are

P-ACCOUNT-1 independent tx storage-flag order and mint-after-read discipline (2026-08-19)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Branch `cursor/wave1-account1-main-0830-d8f8` from `origin/main`
  `8ae5dcc` (#105, "restyle Verity TX observe-from-storage and restate honest
  claims"). Strengthens the registered `P-ACCOUNT-1` parent so its Verity
  transaction plane no longer shares a step-list bridge with the abstract
  source if-tree, and adds an explicit ordering discipline plus a kill-line
  mutant on the two tick-bearing writes that discipline depends on.
- `LidoSRv3/Audit/Verity/HandleOracleReportTx.lean`'s `storedSteps` used to
  call `AccountingCorrespondence.successfulSteps` directly inside
  `handleOracleReport`'s own `Result`/`View` construction, so the tx plane's
  observed steps and the abstract source's step list were, structurally, the
  same function evaluated once. `storedSteps` is now a standalone
  reconstruction that reads three tick-tagged storage flags
  (`accountingCalledSlot`, `rewardsReadSlot`, `rewardsMintedSlot`, written as
  the literals `1`, `2`, `3` at their call sites) directly off the `dirty`
  `ContractState`, and never calls `successfulSteps`. The correspondence
  theorem `verity_tx_simulates_pinned_source` is otherwise unchanged in
  statement; only its supporting `storedSteps` plumbing moved off the shared
  helper.
- New predicate `mintAfterRead readTick mintTick := 0 < mintTick.val →
  readTick.val < mintTick.val` is a second, independent check over the two
  raw ticks, not a presence check: `ContractState` writes commute, so
  `storedSteps`'s "is this flag equal to its literal" test cannot see write
  order by itself. `mintAfterReadDisciplineOf` states the property for an
  arbitrary tick-writing transaction and `mintAfterReadDiscipline_holds`
  proves it for the real `handleOracleReport` (ticks `2 < 3`, unconditionally
  true regardless of the fee premise).
- `handleOracleReportSwappedMintBeforeRead` is a second executable
  transaction, defined beside the discipline it violates, that writes the
  mint tick (`2`) at the call site that used to write the read tick, and the
  read tick (`3`) at the call site that used to write the mint tick. The
  kill-line theorem `mintOrderKillLine_holds` proves this mutant does not
  satisfy `mintAfterReadDisciplineOf`: on an empty positive-fee report the
  swapped ticks are `mint = 2`, `read = 3`, so `mintAfterRead 3 2` reduces to
  `0 < 2 → 3 < 2`, which is `False`.
- `PAccount1.mint_after_read_discipline` and `PAccount1.mint_order_kill_line`
  re-export both theorems on the registered guarantee facade, alongside the
  pre-existing `source_report_before_reward`,
  `verity_tx_simulates_oracle_report`, and
  `verity_tx_revert_restores_snapshot`. No existing theorem statement was
  weakened; the two new ones are additive.
- `LidoSRv3/Tests/HandleOracleReportTxMutants.lean`: `bypassOrderGuard` is
  updated to the tick semantics (writing `2`/`3` and reading back through
  `storedSteps` instead of `successfulSteps`); a new `mintUnconditionally`
  mutant always writes the mint tick regardless of the fee premise and is
  shown (by `example`/`native_decide` on a zero-fee input) to diverge from
  `sourceView`, i.e. the fees-conditional the parent depends on is load-bearing
  and its removal is mechanically caught. The swapped-order mutant example is
  split into a concrete-tick-value check and a separate manual proof of the
  `mintAfterRead` violation (`native_decide` alone could not synthesize a
  `Decidable` instance for the implication `0 < mintTick → readTick <
  mintTick`); `swapped_mint_read_kill_line_refutes_parent` connects that test
  witness to `mintOrderKillLine_holds` on the registered parent.
- Disclosed, not silently narrowed: `storedSteps`'s presence check still
  cannot see a mutation that reorders the three `writeSlot` call sites while
  keeping each tick literal pinned to its original slot (writes to distinct
  slots commute in `ContractState`). `mint_after_read_discipline` /
  `mint_order_kill_line` catch only the narrower case where the tick *value*
  travels with the reordered call site. `report/P-ACCOUNT-1.md` issue 5 is
  updated to state this residual explicitly; it is not claimed closed.
  `report/P-ACCOUNT-1.md` issue 11 is also corrected to say
  `rewardsMintedSlot` now writes tick `3`, not the stale `1`.
- `audit/guarantees.yaml`'s `P-ACCOUNT-1` row is updated: `reproduction.expected`,
  `summary`, `fidelity.covered`, `classification.work`, and `next_gate` all
  name the new tick-based storage-flag independence and the mint-after-read
  discipline/kill-line pair. `classification.kind` remains
  `IMPLEMENTATION_PENDING`; the row is not promoted to `NONE` because the
  `fidelity.missing` list (SRStorage membership, caller/role,
  `submitReportData`, fee computation, packed accounting words, re-read of
  the router snapshot) is unchanged and nonempty.
  `scripts/audit_metadata.py`'s `EXPECTED_CANONICAL_DETAIL_SHA256["P-ACCOUNT-1"]`
  is recomputed to match. `audit/ROADMAP.md`, `audit/STATUS.md`, and
  `audit/REPRODUCE.md` are regenerated with `make audit-generate`; the diff is
  confined to the `P-ACCOUNT-1` row.

P-RESERVE-RELATIONAL checked locked-ETH accumulator (2026-08-18)
----------------------------------------------------------------

- Closes the single `fidelity.missing` entry that held the row at
  `IMPLEMENTATION_PENDING` in the "P-RESERVE-RELATIONAL executable
  finalization composition" section below. The fix is in the executable
  transaction, not in metadata.
- `LidoSRv3.Audit.Verity.ReserveRelationalTx.finalize` implements the Solidity
  0.8 checked `lockedEtherAmount += prefinalized`. Where the accumulator would
  leave one word it now returns `.revert "LOCKED_ETH_OVERFLOW"` on the exact
  pre-call snapshot; previously it stored the wrapped word and committed. The
  guard is `if Verity.Core.MAX_UINT256 < locked`, so the boundary is
  inclusive: landing exactly on `MAX_UINT256` still commits.
- The complementary regime is proved, not left implicit.
  `PReserveRelationalVerity.verity_tx_reverts_on_locked_overflow` shows that
  for every decode and every `txRun` whose accumulator exceeds one word, the
  transaction reverts on the entry state. Together with the correspondence
  theorem this covers both regimes; neither is a hole.
- The correspondence theorem
  `verity_tx_simulates_reserve_relational_spec` now carries an explicit
  `NoLockedOverflow inputs before` premise. That side condition is stated over
  *committed* pinned-source runs only (`sourceRun inputs before = .committed
  after observables → observables.lockedEtherAfter ≤ MAX_UINT256`), which is
  exactly the regime in which the checked addition succeeds, so it is neither
  vacuous nor wider than the arithmetic it stands for. It is discharged in
  proof from the witnessed committed run rather than assumed as an axiom.
- The relational claim itself is unconditional.
  `verity_reserve_does_not_change_finalization` takes no overflow premise: two
  worlds differing only in `depositsReserve` decode to the same locked ETH and
  therefore overflow or commit alike, so non-interference holds across both
  regimes. This is the property the row exists to state, and it was not
  weakened to obtain the closure.
- `A-CLASSICAL-CHOICE` is now disclosed in `audit/assumptions.yaml` and named
  in the `LidoSRv3.Audit.Trust` allowed-axiom doc-comment. It records that
  `Classical.choice` is accepted alongside `propext` and `Quot.sound` as a
  kernel-discharged foundational axiom, so the gate keeps distinguishing it
  from genuine escapes (`sorryAx`, `Lean.ofReduceBool`, project-level
  `axiom`). It is a disclosure of what the gate already allowed, not a new
  trust concession.
- Mutant vectors pin the new behaviour mechanically, over `Contract.run` and
  not over hand-written lists: an accumulator that would leave one word
  reverts; the revert is the checked-arithmetic one and restores the exact
  snapshot; the rejection is a real wrap rather than an unreachable guard
  (`(word (MAX_UINT256 + 80)).val = 79`); a wrapping mutant that stores the
  truncated word is observably distinguished; and the inclusive boundary
  commits. All use `rfl` or `decide`; no `native_decide`.
- Three proof repairs were needed on top of the authored fix, all in
  `ReserveRelationalTx.lean` and all in tactic scripts rather than statements.
  In `verity_reserve_does_not_change_finalization` the three post-guard
  branches omitted the `hRun` case hypothesis, so the `txRun` match never
  reduced. In `verity_tx_simulates_pinned_source` an `rw [hEnds] at hNoWrap`
  fired before the `unfold` that exposes `batchEnds` (a correctly ordered
  duplicate already followed it), and `refine hNoWrap _ _ ?_` left the
  observables metavariable undetermined; the committed-run witness is now
  supplied explicitly. No statement, premise, or conclusion changed.

P-DEPOSIT-1 composed parent-transaction repair (2026-08-18)
-----------------------------------------------------------

- `LidoSRv3/Audit/Verity/DepositParentTx.lean` runs the pinned two-batch
  `StakingRouter.deposit` path as one `Verity.Contract.run` program:
  authorization, module-active and allocation guards, the reentrancy counter,
  per-module `writeMapUint` allocation / dynamic-data / deposit-data-root
  writes, the Lido pull, and both beacon value legs through
  `externalCallBindTo` frames carrying concrete targets, wei values and
  argument words.
- `execute_observes_source` quantifies over every `Inputs` and every entry
  `ContractState` satisfying `Preconditions` and is proved by reducing the
  program, not by `decide` on a ground term. Observables expose the per-module
  allocation, dynamic-data and deposit-data-root words alongside the
  conservation aggregates, the guard counter, the Lido ledger, the router's
  closing retention, and the ordered call journal down to name, destination,
  wei value and argument words.
- `PDeposit1.verity_tx_composes_deposit_conservation_and_rollback` is a single
  theorem over shared variables `(cfg, inp, inputs, entry)`. `LinksSource`
  pins the transaction inputs to the pinned-source deposit quantities, so the
  abstract conservation/rollback claim, the executable revert boundary, the
  source-observable correspondence, and the cross-plane `pulled`/`pushed`
  equality all range over the same data. `canonical_composition_witness`
  discharges `LinksSource` and `Preconditions` on a deployment where the
  source model commits, so the composition is not vacuous.
- `LidoSRv3/Tests/DepositParentTxMutants.lean` mutates `execute` itself,
  pinned to production by `mutant_none_reproduces_execute`. Mutants cover
  skipped per-module allocation, dynamic-data and root writes, a skipped
  counter bump, a skipped Lido debit, and dropped, misrouted, mis-valued,
  reordered and duplicated beacon pushes, plus intermediate-write rollback.
- P-DEPOSIT-1 is registered in `LidoSRv3/Audit/AllGuarantees.lean` and its
  theorems are listed in `LidoSRv3/Audit/Trust.lean`; every one depends only
  on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`.
- Verity is pinned to `a063bfc869735045354ebc3862ca08859da0f56e`, EVMYulLean to
  `f7e4ee0dc8f8d5265ce822a937ab5be771f182e9`, and Lido core to
  `af095e48bbc1c3841c2c9936219c8461af01056b`. This lane was certified against
  Verity `8719dc52e8c581e37bab4594fed11b11c41494b8` and re-verified after the
  merge that advanced the pin.

  `validated-tree` above.

P-TOPUP-1 external-call-frame repair of the faithful plane (2026-08-18)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------

- Repairs the three defects the PR #96 review recorded at `ed79b7b`. Each is
  addressed in the executable transaction, not in metadata.
- Defect 1 (circular journal). The prior `callPass` credited `selfBalance` by
  hand and appended a preconstructed `expectedCalls` list that the
  specification side also consumed, so no mutation of the call sequence could
  be detected. `LidoSRv3.Audit.Verity.TopupTx.execute` now performs the Lido
  pull and every per-validator beacon deposit through Verity's merged
  external-call frame `Contracts.externalCallBindTo` (upstream #2362). The
  journal in `ContractState.calls` is therefore produced by running the
  transaction; `execute` never mentions `expectedCalls`. The frame's
  `value <= selfBalance` check is load-bearing rather than decorative: the
  duplicated-deposit mutant overspends the pull and reverts with
  `insufficient balance` instead of minting wei.
- Defect 2 (aggregate-only observables). `observe` previously exposed only
  `pulled`, `pushed`, and `allocationTotalSlot`. It now additionally exposes
  the journal's call names, call targets, call values, and ABI argument words
  in execution order, together with the per-validator allocation mapping words
  read back through `readMapUint`. The composed theorem
  `PTopup1.verity_tx_simulates_source` equates that widened observable with
  `sourceObservables`, which is derived from the pinned-source push schedule.
- Defect 3 (mutants over pure lists). `LidoSRv3.Tests.TopupTxMutants` no longer
  compares hand-written journal lists. It defines `mutantExecute`, which runs
  the same `allocationStage` writes and the same `externalCallBindTo` frames
  through `Contract.run`. `mutant_none_reproduces_execute` pins the unmutated
  mutant to production `execute` by `rfl`, so the six rejections are rejections
  of a mutated execution: skipped per-validator allocation words (aggregate
  bookkeeping preserved), a dropped deposit, a deposit misrouted to Lido with
  the wei total unchanged, a one-wei short payment, a reordered batch, and a
  replayed first deposit.
- Rollback is proven after real prefix effects, not merely asserted.
  `allocation_write_prefix_is_real`, `lido_pull_prefix_is_real`, and
  `first_push_prefix_is_real` exhibit the dirtied storage word, the journalled
  pull frame, and the journalled first deposit plus its balance debit; the
  three matching `*_rolls_back` theorems then show `Contract.run` returns the
  exact entry snapshot, and `reverted_batch_observes_nothing` shows a reverted
  batch exposes no storage or journal observable.
- Stated side conditions, carried explicitly and not discharged silently:
  `A-TOPUP-NOWRAP` (`allocSum allocations < 2^256`) and
  `hLen : allocations.length <= 2^256` for allocation-mapping key
  injectivity. `A-SOURCE-SHAPED` and `A-VERITY-SCAFFOLD` are unchanged.
- `lake build LidoSRv3.Audit.Verity.TopupTx
  LidoSRv3.Audit.Guarantees.PTopup1 LidoSRv3.Tests.TopupTxMutants`: exit 0,
  no errors and no warnings in the three changed files.
- `lake build LidoSRv3.Audit.AllGuarantees LidoSRv3.Audit.Trust`: exit 0.
  `PTopup1.verity_tx_simulates_source` reports
  `depends on axioms: [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. No `sorry`,
  `admit`, project-level `axiom`, or `native_decide` is introduced by the three
  changed files; the mutant vectors use `decide` and `rfl` only. `Trust.lean`
  now prints axioms for the eight replacement mutant theorems.

  `python3 scripts/check_validation_receipt.py`, and `git diff --check`:
  exit 0.

P-CONSOLIDATION-ETH-1 parent composition closure (2026-08-18)
------------------------------------------------

- Verity is re-pinned from `8719dc52e8c581e37bab4594fed11b11c41494b8` to merged
  upstream `main` `a063bfc869735045354ebc3862ca08859da0f56e`, which contains
  PR #2362 (external-call frames) and PR #2365 (atomic compiled multicall). The
  lakefile request, resolved manifest `rev`/`inputRev`, artifact lock, audit
  manifest, source map, provenance fixture, and checkout all agree on that SHA.
- `LidoSRv3.Audit.Guarantees.PConsolidationEth1.eth_flow_parent` closes the abstract plane
  over `EthPath`, whose three constructors are the complete ETH-bearing
  call-site inventory. For every path it proves no wei reaches a lateral
  destination and that the committed trace totals exactly the entering value.
- `LidoSRv3.Audit.Verity.PConsolidationEth1CompositionTx.run` dispatches the compiled
  Bus/Gateway/Vault ensemble recursively over one shared
  `MultiContract.MultiWorld`. Each hop is a real PR #2362 frame: `callFunction`
  enters the callee's `FunctionSpec`, the body executes under
  `denoteFunctionWithCalls`, and the resulting control decides commit or
  restore. The dispatcher harvests the journal entries the executed body
  appended and pushes them as the next frames, so the call graph is emitted by
  the caller bodies rather than assembled by hand.
- ETH moves exactly once per hop under a declared-amount convention: every
  `LinkedExternal` carries `value := 0`, the body-computed amount travels as
  calldata word `0`, the dispatcher lifts it into `CallSite.value`, and every
  receiver opens with `require(msg.value == amount)`.
- `verity_tx_composes_value_flow_and_rollback` is stated over `TxView` outcome
  observables -- control, hop count, and the seven-account balance sheet -- not
  over full contract states. It covers the fee/refund split for one- and
  two-request batches and the exact-fee batch, restoration of the entry world
  when the request predeploy rejects, the in-Gateway revert of an underfunded
  batch, ETH conservation on success and on revert, and equality between the
  recursively dispatched run and a PR #2365 `denoteTransaction` replay of the
  discovered `List CompiledCall`.
- `LidoSRv3.Tests.PConsolidationEth1CompositionTxMutants` runs the same ensemble and the same
  dispatcher under a mutated `Wiring`, so no mutant can be killed by weakening
  the parent statement. The five mutants are drop (`emitRefund := false`),
  misroute (`vaultTarget := lidoAddr`), corrupt (`refundWholeValue := true`),
  rollback (`observeWithoutRollback`), and two-batch
  (`perRequestCalls := false`). The rollback mutant is falsifiable rather than
  tautological because `TxOutcome` retains both `entryWorld` and `lastWorld`.
- `lake env lean LidoSRv3/Audit/Trust.lean`: `eth_flow_parent`,
  `verity_tx_composes_value_flow_and_rollback`, and all five mutants report
  `depends on axioms: [propext, Quot.sound]`. The decision procedure is
  `decide +kernel`, which is kernel reduction and introduces no axiom; no
  `sorry`, `admit`, `native_decide`, or project-level axiom is introduced.
- `lake build LidoSRv3.Audit.Trust LidoSRv3.Audit.AllGuarantees`: exit 0,
  `Build completed successfully (1290 jobs)` under Lean 4.31.0.
- `P-CONSOLIDATION-ETH-1` is promoted to abstract `CHECKED` / Verity `CHECKED` with a real
  `P-CONSOLIDATION-ETH-1` source-map target covering all six inventory spans, so the former
  `P-CONSOLIDATION-ETH-1` exemption in `scripts/audit_metadata.py` is removed rather than
  tolerated. `P-CONSOLIDATION-ETH-1a` and `P-CONSOLIDATION-ETH-1b` stay bounded per-leg children.
- Standing scope is unchanged: the parent is a model-plane composition under
  `A-SOURCE-SHAPED`, `A-VERITY-SCAFFOLD`, and `A-ABSTRACT-TX`. It does not claim
  the Bus, Gateway, or Vault are the pinned Solidity contracts compiled to
  bytecode, nor that these are executable EVM trace semantics. Identifying the
  immutable `CONSOLIDATION_REQUEST` value with the canonical EIP-7251 address
  remains the separate `P-CONSOLIDATION-ETH-1b` provenance obligation.
- Receipt subject: tracked tree excluding this receipt,
  `78e69c3ec590add55c38a8589a473d969b5f3263`.

  updates. The canonical transaction theorems require the transaction row to
  be the row returned by `Source.candidate?`.
- The typed `Contract.run` slice proves checked arithmetic, strict progress,
  capacity non-breach, accumulator/remaining-demand updates, and exact
  snapshot rollback. Candidate-array memory semantics, Yul, EVM, runtime
  bytecode, and deployment provenance remain open.
- `A-ALLOC2-TX-BOUNDARY` records that candidate scanning, candidate count, and
  allocation-size upper bound are supplied as source-plane words.

P-TOPUP-2 bounded source/transaction closure (2026-08-15)
---------------------------------------------------------

- P-TOPUP-2 closes its SOURCE and TX planes: the registry theorem moves to
  `LidoSRv3.Audit.Verity.Topup2Tx.tx_aggregate_bounded_by_block_cap`, which
  binds the gateway's value-bearing top-up schedule to the pinned Verity

  bounds the observed call-value aggregate by the block cap for every
  `AdversaryModel` and initial call state, conditional on an explicit
  runtime-provenance witness. Supporting theorems cover exact conservation on
  full success, revert restoring the initial world, commit as the adversary
  transition fold, and revert provenance from an observed failed call.

  Verity pin `04729a9de9099e065dd09283e4f733a5fd4c2a16` that combinator is
  `pure ()`, so a conservation statement over it would be vacuous. The module
  docstring records this.

P-ALLOC-1 Phase-3 exact-head repair (2026-08-13)
---------------------------------------------------

- The bounded `SRLib._getStakingModuleSummary` slice now records its selector
  as the four calldata bytes `9a bd df 09`, with a `DenoteMemory` selector-word
  theorem rather than a one-word pseudo-payload.
- The real P-ALLOC-1 facade, `AllGuarantees`, the canonical guarantee metadata,
  trust report, and target manifest consume the same bounded transaction
  theorem. The public minimal-11 list and `campaign/lido-minimal-11` are not
  changed.
- The executable Verity wrapper writes a model-local scalar storage word before
  executing the exact mapped `CallProgram` observation inside `Contract.run`.
  Its target, selector calldata, call kind, gas, and result are retained by the
  non-circular adapter bridge; a target-mutation regression makes that observed
  call revert and restores the pre-call snapshot. This is not a Yul, EVM,
  deployed-layout, or full allocation-loop theorem.
- Focused regressions reject selector, call-kind, return-layout, and pre-call
  storage mutations, and assert the failed typed-call transaction snapshot.
- The follow-up exact-head repair evaluates the mapped observation against the
  post-store `ContractState` and derives its `Contract.run` revert directly
  from the observed mapped-call `.revert` result. Focused Phase-3, canonical
  P-ALLOC-1, allocation regression, and semantic-mutant targets: exit 0.
  The canonical theorem's axiom report is printed by `Audit.Trust`. This
  receipt records bounded MODEL → SOURCE → VERITY_TX evidence only; it does
  not claim allocation-loop, Yul, EVM, deployed-storage-layout, or runtime
  provenance closure.
- The final exact-head repair retains successful-call returndata, rejects a
  response shorter than 96 bytes, decodes the copied capacity word at offset
  64, and rejects a capacity above the pre-call `depositable` value. Executable
  regressions cover both rejecting branches and snapshot rollback. The focused
  reproduction command, aggregate guarantees, trust report, metadata mutants,
  and full `lake build LidoSRv3` all exit 0 under Lean 4.31.0.
- The current exact-head repair removes that invented capacity-versus-request
  rejection from both the source-shaped body and executable adapter: a complete
  summary whose capacity exceeds this call's `depositable` value now succeeds,
  while malformed short returndata still reverts and restores the pre-call
  snapshot. This remains bounded MODEL → SOURCE → VERITY_TX evidence only;
  it does not claim allocation-loop, Yul, EVM, deployed-layout, or runtime
  provenance closure.
- Canonical and supplemental P-ALLOC-1 guarantee facades consume that corrected
  theorem shape. Focused Phase-3 and aggregate P-ALLOC-1 builds, plus the full
  `lake build LidoSRv3`, exit 0 under Lean 4.31.0.
- The proof-report guard now checks the resolved Verity revision before any
  `lake env` invocation, so Lake cannot repair a stale checkout before the
  negative provenance regression observes and rejects it.

- Independent review found that
  `admission_and_post_state_equivariance` only conjoined admission and
  post-state properties supplied as hypotheses; it did not prove either
  property for a modeled permissionless entrypoint.
- P-ADDRESS-1 is therefore no longer recorded as MODEL- or transaction-checked.
  Its registry checked-layer list is empty, its metadata theorem evidence is
  removed, and the address-renaming relation remains a specification helper.
- Restoring MODEL closure requires instantiating eligible permissionless
  entrypoints and proving both properties without assuming them.

  overload lines 110--118 and root overload lines 120--135,

  `source_pinned_config_discharges_deposit_data_root` reports only Lean
  foundations `propext` and `Quot.sound`.
- `python3 scripts/audit_metadata.py check`: exit 0.
- `PYTHONOPTIMIZE=1 python3 scripts/test_audit_metadata.py`: exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/check_validation_receipt.py`: exit 0 after this receipt's
  `validated-tree` field is bound to the final tracked tree.
- `bash scripts/check_no_python_evidence.sh`: exit 0.
- The opaque `sha256` boundary now returns `Sha256Digest`, a wrapper carrying
  the pinned `bytes32` shape (`widthPinned` and `bytesBounded`) rather than an
  unconstrained `Bytes`. The cryptographic contents stay unmodelled; what the
  wrapper pins is only the ABI shape the Solidity source guarantees, and it is
  what forces every nested `abi.encodePacked` preimage in
  `_computeSignatureRoot` and `_computeDepositDataRootWithAmount` to the
  source's 64-byte layout. Those widths are stated and proved as
  `signaturePart1_length`, `signaturePart2_preimage_length`,
  `signatureRoot_preimage_length`, `publicKeyRoot_preimage_length`,
  `publicKeyWithdrawal_preimage_length`, `amountSignature_preimage_length`,
  and `depositDataRoot_preimage_length`.
- The structural leaf `sourceLeaf` now binds the deposit-data-root *digest
  value* through `digestNode`, the positional base-257 encoding of the digest
  bytes, instead of the digest length. `digestBytesFold_injective` and
  `digestNode_injective` prove that encoding injective on pinned-width byte
  lists, and `sourceLeaf_injective` carries the consequence at the leaf level:
  two inputs sharing a witness leaf share the whole deposit-data-root digest,
  so a mutated root cannot reuse the witness leaf. No injectivity is claimed
  for the composite `sourceNode`. The constant pinned width is kept as the
  separate `sourceNodeWidth`, discharged by `sourceNodeWidth_pinned`.
- `LidoSRv3.Tests.SszRegression` carries the corresponding mutant regressions.
  Replacing the value-bearing projection with the constant length projection
  makes `digestNode (markedDigest 1) ≠ digestNode (markedDigest 2)` fail
  (verified by mutation, with the injectivity lemma stubbed so the mutant still
  elaborates), while the length projection cannot tell those two pinned-width
  digests apart. Weakening the wrapper's `widthPinned` field to an
  unconstrained result rejects `concatDigests_length`, the six preimage-width
  lemmas, and the `digest.widthPinned` regression in the test module.
- The public guarantee surface is narrowed, not widened:
  `LidoSRv3/Audit/Guarantees/PSsz1.lean` now exposes exactly one theorem,
  `structural_witness_binding_sound`, alongside `guarantee` and a docstring
  recording what the facade does not claim. The multi-conjunct
  `source_pinned_config_discharges_deposit_data_root` is no longer restated
  through the facade; it is stated and proved once, in
  `LidoSRv3/Audit/Source/DepositDataRootCorrespondence.lean`, next to the source
  shape it constrains, with each conjunct annotated by the pinned source span it
  discharges. The statement and the proof are unchanged and the theorem still
  reports only Lean foundations `propext` and `Quot.sound`.
  `LidoSRv3.Audit.Trust` and the
  `verity/targets/audit-manifest.json` theorem ledger now name it at its
  `Source.DepositDataRootCorrespondence` path instead of the removed
  `Guarantees.PSsz1` alias. No status, catalogue wording, source-map span,
  manifest pin, or toolchain revision was changed, and `audit/guarantees.yaml`
  still carries the `P-SSZ-1.deposit-data-root` row, which already pointed at
  the `Source.DepositDataRootCorrespondence` theorem.
- Scope is raw-signature source-shaped control flow and a nonconstant structural
  witness binding over a value-bearing, fixed-width digest; a wrong bound root
  is rejected by `SszRegression`. SHA-256 correctness and precompile semantics
  remain `STRETCH_OPAQUE_FFI`; EVM, canonical runtime provenance, and

  builds -- `LidoSRv3.Tests.MinFirstVectors`, `.SszRegression`,
  `.DepositVectors`, and `.TopupVectors`, the last of which pins this gate's
  top-up conservation and rollback claims -- and all five reference-fixture
  presence checks. As with `make prove`, this is a toolchain-availability
  regression rather than a proof or vector regression.
- `python3 scripts/audit_metadata.py check`: exit 0.
- Changed-Lean forbidden proof-escape scan: exit 0, with no new
  `sorry`, `admit`, `axiom`, or `unsafe` declaration/tactic.
- Unlike P-DEPOSIT-1, top-up conservation is *unconditional*: the pull
  accumulated at `StakingRouter.sol` line 732 and the push summed at
  `BeaconChainDepositor.sol` line 106 are two readings of the same `_amounts`
  array, not a key count scaled by two separately configured constants. The
  line 755 balance `assert` is therefore discharged rather than load-bearing,
  and `source_balance_guards_discharged` states that both the assert failure
  and the insufficient-router-balance revert are unreachable. Both `Outcome`
  constructors are retained rather than deleted, because that unreachability
  is conditioned on the accumulation in the `unchecked` block opened at source
  line 722 not wrapping; a 2^256 wrap there would make them live again.
- That no-wrap fact is carried explicitly, not implicitly: it is the
  `SolidityTopup.NoUncheckedWrap` hypothesis on
  `source_balance_guards_discharged`, and it is recorded as `A-TOPUP-NOWRAP` in
  `audit/assumptions.yaml` and linked from P-TOPUP-1 in `audit/guarantees.yaml`.
  It is not derivable from the pinned P-TOPUP-1 spans: the per-index cap at
  source line 728 originates in `TopUpGateway` (P-TOPUP-2) and the key count is
  unbounded on this path. Its proved half is
  `source_unchecked_accumulation_faithful`, which shows that under exactly that
  hypothesis the source's `unchecked` accumulation at line 732 and the model's
  `Nat` sum are the same number, so the discharge is a statement about the
  source rather than only about the model. The negative fixture suite rejects
  dropping `A-TOPUP-NOWRAP` from the P-TOPUP-1 assumption links.
- The transitive helpers that carry the modelled guards are now pinned in
  `audit/source-map.yaml` rather than named only in prose:
  `StakingRouter._checkAppAuth` (lines 1177--1179) and `_getTopUpGateway`
  (lines 1169--1171) behind the line 686 authorization, `_getModuleState`
  (lines 1099--1107) and `SRUtils._requireModuleIdExists` (`SRUtils.sol` lines
  45--47) behind the line 689 module-existence guard, and
  `SRUtils._requireWCType2` (`SRUtils.sol` lines 41--43) behind the line 694
  withdrawal-credentials-type guard. The negative fixture suite rejects
  dropping any one of the five. The predicates inside those helpers
  (`LIDO_LOCATOR.topUpGateway()`, `SRStorage.isModuleExists`,
  `WithdrawalCredentials.isType2`, and the `_msgSender()` comparison) remain
  abstract storage/interface facts taken as model inputs.
- The constant declarations `pinnedConfig` hard-codes are now pinned in
  `audit/source-map.yaml` as well: `StakingRouter.PUBKEY_LENGTH` (line 57),
  `BeaconChainDepositor.PUBLIC_KEY_LENGTH` (line 21) and
  `BeaconChainDepositor.MIN_DEPOSIT` (line 28). Without them the source-map
  gate stayed green while a change to either pubkey-length constant would have
  invalidated `source_pinned_config_discharges_pubkey_guard`, which holds only
  while the two agree. The negative fixture suite rejects dropping any one of
  the three. `pinnedConfig.gwei` needs no declaration span (it is the Solidity
  unit literal `1 gwei`) and `pinnedConfig.uint64Max` is `type(uint64).max`,
  read at `BeaconChainDepositor.sol` line 97 inside the already-pinned lines
  66--108.
- The vectors pin `_checkAppAuth(_getTopUpGateway())` at source line 686 as the
  *first* guard, so no unauthorized call is classified as a committed outcome
  whatever the later module status is, and pin the zero-total early commit at
  source line 741 as a *commit* rather than a revert, which is why
  `committedNoTopUp_is_not_a_rollback` is stated explicitly.
- Source plane only: `evm` remains OPEN and `tx` remains
  ABSTRACT_LEAN_CHECKED for P-TOPUP-1. The per-validator top-up amount
  computation and limit accounting (P-TOPUP-2), the allocation algorithm
  producing `_amounts` (P-ALLOC-1/2), and the SSZ deposit-data-root
  construction at `BeaconChainDepositor.sol` lines 120--146 (P-SSZ-1) stay out
  of scope.
- `make prove` was NOT run to completion on this tree: the workspace Lean shim
  cannot execute `lake env lean --version` (remote exit 126, "Permission
  denied"), which is the provenance stamp the `prove` recipe emits before
  `lake build LidoSRv3`. The failure reproduces identically for `make
  bootstrap` on an unmodified checkout, so it is a toolchain-availability
  regression rather than a proof regression; the `lake build LidoSRv3` half of
  the same recipe completed successfully.

P-RESERVE-RELATIONAL executable finalization composition (2026-08-18)
---------------------------------------------------------------------

- Authored on `prove/p-reserve-relational-2` from `main`
  `54db83e0` with no rebase, force-push, or merge. Slice 1 supplied the
  abstract statement only; this slice adds the executable plane and is the
  first time the parent is registered.
- `LidoSRv3.Audit.Verity.ReserveRelationalTx.finalize` is an executable
  `Contract.run` transaction, not a wrapper over the interpreter. It decodes
  the requested batch ends from a denoted `uint256[]` memory array through
  `evalExpr (.memoryArrayElement ...)`, reads queue economics from four
  `mapUint` storage channels behind an existence marker, journals every
  prefinalized endpoint, and persists locked ETH and the last finalized
  request id with `writeSlot`. It computes all five observables:
  `prefinalizedRanges`, `prefinalizedEth`, `sharesToBurn`, `finalizedRange`,
  and `lockedEtherAfter`.
- `verity_tx_simulates_reserve_relational_spec` composes the three planes: the
  transaction's committed/reverted observables equal those of the
  independently defined pinned-source interpreter, which in turn corresponds
  to the abstract spec via `source_run_correspondence`. The composition is
  non-vacuous: `Decodes (worldFor inputs lowReserve) inputs lowReserve` is
  discharged by `rfl` on a concrete storage-and-memory world.
- `verity_reserve_slot_is_not_read` proves `depositsReserveSlot` lies outside
  the transaction's read frame, including under an injected post-write
  failure, and `verity_reserve_does_not_change_finalization` states the
  relational guarantee on the executable plane.
- `verity_revert_restores_snapshot` proves that any failure, including one
  injected after the batch journal and both finalization writes, returns the
  exact pre-transaction snapshot.
- Verity-plane mutants reject a reserve-to-range read, a reserve-to-shares
  read, and an off-by-one range endpoint; report, queue-pause, unknown batch
  end, and buffer-shortfall paths fail closed; the discount column and a
  two-call batch chain are pinned. No `native_decide` is used anywhere in this
  lane: every vector closes by `rfl` or `decide`.
- Recorded fidelity gap: the abstract plane is unbounded `Nat`, so the
  transaction stores the wrapped `uint256` word on the locked ETH accumulator
  where Solidity 0.8 checked addition would revert. This is the single
  `fidelity.missing` entry, which holds the row at
  `IMPLEMENTATION_PENDING`; no fully-checked claim is made.
  SUPERSEDED on 2026-08-18 by the checked-accumulator closure recorded in
  "P-RESERVE-RELATIONAL checked locked-ETH accumulator" above: the gap is
  closed, `fidelity.missing` is empty, and the row is `NONE`.
- Registration is post-composition only. The canonical minimal-11 facade,
  its length regression, and its ID-order regression are untouched;
  `P-RESERVE-RELATIONAL` is a supplemental row in `AllGuarantees.supplemental`
  and the 14th subordinate metadata row.
- Focused Lean build, `LidoSRv3.Audit.AllGuarantees`, and
  `LidoSRv3.Audit.Trust`: exit 0, 1289 jobs. The six registered
  `PReserveRelational` theorems report only `[propext]`, `[propext,
  Quot.sound]`, or `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`; no `sorryAx` and
  no `Lean.ofReduceBool` appear in the axiom gate.
- `make test`, `python3 scripts/audit_metadata.py check` (11 canonical
  guarantees plus 14 subordinate rows), the optimized metadata mutants,
