  `scripts/check_public_claim_surfaces.py`,

Wave 4: P-ADDRESS-1 vacuous-parent-hypothesis remediation (2026-08-19)
----------------------------------------------------------------------

- Defect (wave-4 review finding, coordinator-verified): the registered
  parent `LidoSRv3.Audit.Guarantees.PAddress1.universal_address_writer_equivariance`
  carried `_hScope : addressEquivarianceEntryScope inp.entryPoint`,
  `_hWellFormed : wellFormedAddressInput inp`, and
  `_hNotSingleton : ¬ singletonActorEntryPoint inp.entryPoint` as
  underscore-prefixed binders never referenced by the proof body -- three
  decorative premises. `_hNotSingleton` was additionally content-free:
  `singletonActorEntryPoint` is defined as `False` for every `EntryPoint`
  in this model (`not_singleton_actor_entry_point` proves the negation
  vacuously), so requiring it restricted nothing while reading as a real
  exclusion. `audit/guarantees.yaml`'s summary and `fidelity.covered`
  overclaimed a "scoped, well-formed ... explicit singleton-actor
  exclusion" parent that Lean never had.
- Fix: ports the un-landed wave-3 branch commit `b07e40b`
  (`cursor/wave3-paddress1-0830`; PR #131 was closed after only the
  kill-line half merged, so the hypothesis half never reached `main`).

Wave 4: P-DEPOSIT-1 definitional-conjunct strip and source-plane kill-line (2026-08-19)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Fixes P-DEPOSIT-1: the registered parent
  `source_deposit_conserves_and_rolls_back`
  (`LidoSRv3/Audit/Guarantees/PDeposit1.lean`) had a definitional rollback
  conjunct and a mostly-vacuous conservation conjunct, and its cited
  kill-line refuted a Verity-plane sibling predicate rather than the
  registered abstract parent's own predicate on a mutant of its own model.
  The rollback conjunct was definitional: `observation`
  (`LidoSRv3/Audit/Source/DepositCorrespondence.lean`) maps `o.reverts` to
  `.reverted` by construction, and `TxObservation.committedState` /
  `committedTrace` of `.reverted` are `before` / `⟨[], [], []⟩` by
  definition (`LidoSRv3/Audit/Trace.lean`), with `after`, `attempts`, and
  `trace` unconstrained free parameters discarded on the revert branch.
  The conservation conjunct `(run cfg inp).pulled = (run cfg inp).pushed`
  was mostly `0 = 0`: `Outcome.pulled`/`Outcome.pushed` are defined as `0`
  on every non-`committedDeposits` branch, so the universal equality had
  real content only on the assert-gated commit branch. And the cited
  kill-line `DepositParentTxMutants.skipped_lido_debit_breaks_pulled_eq_pushed`
  refuted Verity `DepositParentTx.Observables.pulled = pushed` on a mutant
  Verity execute -- not the registered abstract parent's predicate on a
  mutant of `SolidityDeposit.run`.
- Fix applied: the registered parent keeps its name but its conclusion is
  now two non-definitional conjuncts. (1) Commit-branch-explicit
  conservation: `∀ keys pulled pushed balanceAfter, run cfg inp =
  .committedDeposits keys pulled pushed balanceAfter → pulled = pushed ∧
  depositsValue cfg inp = pushedValue cfg inp` -- the equality between the
  independently written line-972 pull formula and the BeaconChainDepositor
  loop push formula, forced only by the line-996 assert gate, stated on
  the one branch that moves wei. (2) Source-plane rollback: `¬
  ConservingConfig cfg → 0 < actualDepositsCount cfg inp → (run cfg
  inp).reverts = true`, which needs the empty-batch early return excluded

Wave 4: P-ALLOC-2 decorative-hypothesis / sibling kill-line remediation (2026-08-19)
--------------------------------------------------------------

- Fixes P-ALLOC-2: the registered parent
  `PAlloc2.proportional_step_correspondence_and_bounded` declared
  `_hSelected : MinFirstAllocation.Source.candidate? source = some best` but
  its proof term was
  `⟨full_candidate_correspondence hRows, source_amount_totality hOpen hLen hSize hAmount⟩`
  -- the premise was never used, so the first conjunct only related the
  Model and Source candidate scans to *each other* and never to the
  selected row. The "selection kill-line" in
  `Tests/MinFirstDistributionTxMutants.lean` compounded this: it proved
  `firstOpenCandidate? rows ≠ Source.candidate? rows`, a disagreement
  between two Source-side scans with no `Model`, no `RowsCorrespond`, and
  no negation of the parent's correspondence equality -- a sibling
  predicate, not the parent. Only the `checkedAmountNoCapacityCap`
  headroom kill-line actually refuted a parent conjunct, while the YAML
  row and `report/P-ALLOC-2.md` overclaimed that the kill-lines "witness
  both conjuncts".
- Load-bearing fix applied: `hSelected` (underscore dropped) is threaded
  into the parent's first conjunct, which now pins the model scan to the
  selected row:
  `Option.map (fun b => (b.allocation, b.capacity)) (Model.candidate? model)
    = some (best.allocation.val, best.capacity.val)`,
  proved by rewriting `full_candidate_correspondence hRows` with
  `hSelected`. The amount conjuncts (`0 < w.val ∧
  w.val ≤ allocationSize.val ∧ best.allocation.val + w.val ≤
  best.capacity.val`), the theorem name, the remaining hypotheses, and
  every other declaration in `Guarantees/PAlloc2.lean` are unchanged, so
  `EXPECTED_CANONICAL_CLAIMS["P-ALLOC-2"]` still holds.
- New selection kill-line `selection_kill_line_refutes_parent` in
  `Tests/MinFirstDistributionTxMutants.lean`: a mutant Model-side scan
  `mutantFirstOpenCandidate?` (first open bucket wins, replacing the old
  Source-side `firstOpenCandidate?` it subsumes) is refuted on the
  parent's own model. Per coordinator review the theorem is the negation
  of the parent's FULL predicate shape --
  `¬ ∀ {model source best allocationSize w}, RowsCorrespond → hSelected →
  hOpen → hLen → hSize → hAmount → (C1' ∧ C2 ∧ C3 ∧ C4)` with
  `mutantFirstOpenCandidate?` in place of `Model.candidate?` in C1' --
  not a two-premise projection: the witness instantiates all five binders
  and discharges all six premises on concrete data (the `RowsCorrespond`
  pair `[(5, 10), (0, 10)]`; `Source.candidate?` selects the NON-first
  row `⟨w 0, w 10⟩`, index 1, strictly least allocation; the row is open;
  `2 < 2^256`; demand `w 10` nonzero; `Source.checkedAmount` succeeds
  with `some (w 5)`), then contradicts the pinned-selection conjunct,
  which the mutant maps to `some (5, 10)`. The aligned
  `checkedAmountNoCapacityCap` headroom kill-line is unchanged.
- `audit/guarantees.yaml`'s `P-ALLOC-2` row: `summary`,

Wave 4: P-SSZ-1 definitional-conjunct strip and parent-conclusion kill-line (2026-08-19)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Fixes P-SSZ-1: the registered parent `composed_ssz_encoding`
  (`LidoSRv3/Audit/Guarantees/PSsz1.lean`) registered conclusion conjuncts
  that were definitional facts or restated hypotheses, and its two
  kill-lines did not refute the parent's conclusion. The definitional
  conjuncts: `input.rhs.index = (sourceWitness input.src).index.value`,
  `input.digestInput = srcInputs input.src`, and `input.txInput.toInputs =
  srcInputs input.src` are `rfl` accessor equalities of `ComposedSszInput`'s
  own derived fields; `ExactDigestComposition input.digestInput` is `rfl`
  because `digestChain` is defined as that seven-call list; `signatureRoot
  input.src = computeSignatureRoot input.src.signature` is a definitional
  unfolding (`signatureRoot` is defined as that call); the pinned-config
  constant equalities and the `src` field bound projections are constant or
  accessor facts; and the three length lines restated
  `hPublicKey`/`hWithdrawalCredentials`/`hSignature` via `simpa`. The old
  kill-lines refuted only the parent's hypothesis:
  `inconsistent_witness_kill_line` negates `bindOperation ... = true` on a
  crossed two-source pair that cannot even inhabit `ComposedSszInput`, and
  `inconsistent_operation_index_kill_line` is a bare `Nat` constant
  inequality.
- Fix applied: the parent's conclusion is now the named predicate
  `composedEncodingOk operation combine input`, whose every registered
  conjunct is non-definitional: the `hBind`-transported structural witness
  binding soundness; the same derived witness's traversal to `sourceNode
  input.src` under `sourceCombine input.src` (an equality between the
  generic `Ssz.traverseBranch` fold and the independently written pairing
  encoding); `sourceConcat input.lhs input.rhs = specConcat input.lhs
  input.rhs` (two independently written artifacts); the seven-preimage
  count; the `runVerification` accept-iff; and `exactTxWidths
  input.txInput`. The `ComposedSszInput` `rhs`/`digestInput`/`txInput`
  derived-field definitions stay as the type-level one-object coupling;
  their `rfl` equalities are simply no longer registered as claim
  conjuncts.
- New kill-line `crossed_witness_kill_line_refutes_parent` proves
  `¬ composedEncodingOk .clProofVerifier (sourceCombine input.src) input`
  for every `input : ComposedSszInput`: the mutant keeps the honest
  one-object artifact and its own combine but claims the composed encoding
  under the crossed named operation `.clProofVerifier`, and the predicate's
  first structural conjunct is false because `sourceWitness input.src` is
  bound to `.clValidatorVerifier` by construction. A crossed witness/root
  pair inside `ComposedSszInput` is uninhabitable (witness and root are
  both derived from the single `src` -- the type-level coupling doing its
  job), so the operation crossing is the residual mutant shape over exactly
  the operation/combine/input triple the parent quantifies over. The two
  older kill-lines remain with docstrings corrected to state they are
  `bindOperation`-level negative results, not parent-conclusion
  refutations.
  SUPERSEDED in part on 2026-08-19 by "Wave 4: P-SSZ-1 model-mutant parent
  kill-line" below: coordinator review found this theorem is only a
  conclusion-non-triviality witness, not a parent kill-line -- at the

  `swapped_combine_kill_line_refutes_parent` (a `sourceCombine` model

Wave 4: P-SSZ-1 model-mutant parent kill-line (2026-08-19)
----------------------------------------------------------

- Follow-up to the entry above. Coordinator review found that
  `crossed_witness_kill_line_refutes_parent` does not refute the registered
  parent: at the crossed operation `.clProofVerifier` the parent's `hBind`
  hypothesis fails (the witness is bound to `.clValidatorVerifier` by
  construction), so the parent's implication is vacuously true there. The
  theorem is a conclusion-non-triviality witness (the conclusion predicate
  discriminates the claimed operation), not a parent kill-line -- the same
  premise-level shape classified as insufficient for P-RESERVE-1's
  stale-cache theorem.
- Fix applied (the house pattern: mutate the MODEL/FUNCTIONS inside the
  predicate, not the input). The conclusion predicate is now parameterized
  as `composedEncodingOkWithCombine srcCombine operation combine input`
  over the source-combine family used in its deposit-data-root traversal
  conjunct; the registered `composedEncodingOk` is the honest
  specialization at `sourceCombine` (the proposition is unchanged, so the
  registered parent's statement is unchanged). `sourceCombineSwapped`
  (children swapped at every pairing) is the one model function inside the
  predicate that `hBind` never mentions.
- New parent kill-line `swapped_combine_kill_line_refutes_parent`: the
  negation of the mutant-substituted parent stated in the registered
  parent's own quantifier/premise/`hBind` shape,
  `¬ (∀ {operation combine} input, premises → hBind →
  composedEncodingOkWithCombine sourceCombineSwapped operation combine
  input)`. At the concrete witness `swappedCombineKillInput` (pinned
  48/32/96-byte widths, same shape as `Tests/SszRegression.composedExample`;
  defined in the guarantee module since it cannot import the test module)
  every premise holds by `decide` and `hBind` is discharged by the same
  `sourceWitness_binds_sourceNode` used for the honest artifact, while the
  swapped-combine traversal conjunct reduces definitionally
  (`traverseBranch_sourceCombineSwapped_eq`, an `rfl` closed form over the
  witness's concrete `[.right]` path and singleton branch) to a `Nat.pair`
  tree equality that would force `sourceAnchor swappedCombineKillSrc = 0` --
  impossible by repeated `Nat.pair` injectivity

  public-key fold (`digestBytesFold (List.replicate 48 2) ≠ 0`, by
  `decide`). No `sorry`, `admit`, or `native_decide` in the new proofs.

Wave 5: P-RESERVE-1 guard-liveness kill-line (2026-08-19)
---------------------------------------------------------

- Defect (found in wave-5 review): the registered parent
  `PReserve1.source_spend_preserves_withdrawal_reserve` concludes three
  conjuncts -- (1) `scopedWithdrawGuards inputs`, (2)
  `withdrawalPartitionSpendInvariant before after amount`, (3)
  live-reserve invariance -- but conjunct (1) is proved by branch
  inversion on the model's own guard chain and was exercised by no
  kill-line: the wave-4 partition-spend mutant preserves the guards, so
  conjunct (1) holds trivially on its witness (`allowed = ⟨true,true⟩`).
  A conjunct no connected mutant falsifies is dilution (criterion 1
  residual).
- Fix (the P-TOPUP-1 pattern -- a guard-liveness conjunct backed by a
  guard-drop kill-line -- not demotion; the conjunct is real falsifiable
  content about the model's gating): new model mutant
  `mutantWithdrawNoCanDeposit` in `LidoSRv3/Tests/ReserveMutants.lean`, a
  guard-for-guard copy of `modelWithdrawDepositableEther` with ONLY the
  `canDeposit` check dropped (`authorizedRouter`, the nonzero-amount
  check, and the honest `spendDepositableEther` transition unchanged),
  and `guard_drop_kill_line_refutes_parent`: the negation of the
  parent's full predicate shape (all five binders, `freshQueueCache
  before live` retained, same three-conjunct conclusion) with the mutant
  substituted. Witness `guard_drop_mutant_witness`: inputs
  `⟨false, true⟩`, nonzero amount, the standard `vector`, `live = 50`
  (freshness holds by `rfl`); the mutant commits and
  `scopedWithdrawGuards` is false (`canDeposit = false`), so the parent's
  conjunction fails at a premise-satisfying witness. Proof shape:
  `intro`, instantiate, `absurd` off the first conjunct.
- Kill-line division of labor, now explicit in every docstring (the
  parent's docstring, both ReserveMutants section headers, and the
  `staleQueueCacheKillLine` scope note):
  `guard_drop_kill_line_refutes_parent` kills conjunct (1)
  (`scopedWithdrawGuards` guard liveness);
  `partition_spend_mutant_kill_line_refutes_parent` (theorem unchanged)
  kills conjuncts (2)-(3) with freshness retained;
  `staleQueueCacheKillLine_holds` and
  `stale_queue_cache_mutant_counterexample` remain premise-necessity
  evidence for `freshQueueCache`.
- `audit/guarantees.yaml`'s `P-RESERVE-1` row: `summary`,
  `fidelity.covered`, and `reproduction.expected` now cite both
  kill-lines and their division of labor; `abstract.theorem`,

Wave 5: P-ADDRESS-1 parent-shaped kill-line and total entrypoint scope (2026-08-19)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Defect (wave-5 review finding): the kill-line
  `fixed_owner_gate_not_admission_equivariant`
  (`LidoSRv3/Tests/AddressSourceMutants.lean`) had statement shape
  `¬ ∀ a₁ a₂ ≠0 inp, Adm_mutant` -- it negated only the ADMISSION
  conjunct of the fixed-owner-gated mutant, WITHOUT the registered
  parent's `hScope` premise and WITHOUT the post-state equivariance
  conjunct. That is the shape of the sibling
  `source_admission_nondiscriminatory`, not of the registered parent
  `PAddress1.universal_address_writer_equivariance`
  (`∀ a₁ a₂ ≠0 inp, hScope → (Adm ∧ Post)`), so no kill-line refuted
  the registered parent's exact predicate shape (compare the merged
  gold standards `ReserveMutants.partition_spend_mutant_kill_line_refutes_parent`
  and `SolidityConsolidation.fee_blind_commit_kill_line_refutes_parent`).
- Fix (kill-line): new theorem `fixed_owner_gate_kill_line_refutes_parent`
  in `LidoSRv3/Tests/AddressSourceMutants.lean` negates the parent's
  FULL predicate shape on the mutant `runFixedOwnerGated` -- the same
  nonzero-caller binders and BOTH conclusion conjuncts (admission-bit
  equality and committed-post renaming). On the witness
  `fixedOwnerGateWitness` (an otherwise-eligible `requestWithdrawals`
  input, callers `1` = the mutant's fixed owner and `2`), the mutant
  admits caller `1` and commits `⟨1, 1, 1, 1⟩` while the `1 ↔ 2`-swapped
  input reverts (`2 ≠ fixedOwner`), so the admission bits differ AND
  the swapped run cannot commit the renamed post `⟨2, 2, 2, 2⟩`; both
  failures are `decide`-checked in `hBoth` and the refutation closes
  through the post-state conjunct. The admission-only theorem is
  retained with an honest docstring as admission-projection sibling
  evidence, explicitly subsumed by the parent kill-line.
- Strengthening (landed cleanly, no new assumptions):
  `addressEquivarianceEntryScope`
  (`LidoSRv3/Audit/Source/AddressCorrespondence.lean`) now covers
  `claimWithdrawalsTo` too -- its request-owner gate
  (`caller = requestOwner`) is caller-relative and renames with the
  caller (`requestOwner` is an address-indexed input field renamed by
  `renameInput`), so the same source lemmas (`run_rename` and its
  corollaries) apply there. New theorem
  `addressEquivarianceEntryScope_total` proves the scope total over
  the four modeled permissionless writers. The wave-4 `hScope`
  premise thereby became vacuous (`True` on every `EntryPoint`), so
  per the wave-4 load-bearing criterion it is no longer carried by
  the registered parent: `universal_address_writer_equivariance` is
  now unconditional over the four writers, proved by the direct pair
  `source_admission_nondiscriminatory` /
  `source_success_post_state_equivariant` (deleting the binder cannot
  break the proof, and an unused binder would read as a real
  restriction while hiding that the claim is premise-free). The
  kill-line's negated shape correspondingly carries no scope
  implication. `fidelity.missing` drops the `claimWithdrawalsTo`
  exclusion.
- `audit/guarantees.yaml`'s `P-ADDRESS-1` row: `summary`,
  `fidelity.covered`/`missing`, `reproduction.expected`,
  `classification.work`, and `next_gate` now describe the
  unconditional four-writer parent, cite
  `fixed_owner_gate_kill_line_refutes_parent` as the parent kill-line,

  admission-projection sibling evidence. `abstract.theorem`,

  `EXPECTED_CANONICAL_DETAIL_SHA256["P-ADDRESS-1"]` is recomputed to

P-CONSOLIDATION-ETH-1 universal Verity parent (2026-08-21)
--------------------------------------------

- Model change disclosed: the compiled gateway body `gatewayFn` in
  `LidoSRv3/Audit/Verity/PConsolidationEth1CompositionTx.lean` gains the `ZeroArgument`
  guard (`require 0 < msg.value`) this wave, matching the pinned source
  gateway's `ZeroArgument("msg.value")` revert and the abstract plane's
  Wave 1 guard (report/P-CONSOLIDATION-ETH-1.md issue 13). The guard only affects
  `msgValue = 0` runs, which lie outside the registered parent's premises,
  so it neither weakens nor strengthens the registered success claim; it is
  what makes the positivity premise load-bearing (the
  `zero_value_kill_line_refutes_dropped_positivity` witness `(0, 2, 0)`
  reverts at that guard), and it removes the pre-Wave-5 phantom zero-value
  success from the model.
- Lean. The registered Verity parent is lifted from five numeral traces to
  `LidoSRv3.Audit.Guarantees.PConsolidationEth1.verity_tx_universal_success_shape`: for all
  `(msgValue, batchSize, feePerRequest)` that are nonzero-valued, word-sized,
  non-wrapping in the product fee (`batchSize * feePerRequest < 2^256`),
  funded (`batchSize * feePerRequest ≤ msgValue`), and fuel-fit
  (`batchSize + 4 ≤ fuelBudget = 32`), the honest wiring commits with the
  whole product fee at the consolidation-request predeploy, the remainder at
  the refund recipient, and zero retained by every protocol contract on the
  route. Proved in the new module
  `LidoSRv3/Audit/Verity/PConsolidationEth1CompositionTxUniversal.lean` by frame-by-frame
  chaining through the recursive dispatcher, with a `requestPhaseWorld`
  induction over the symbolic batch-size-driven request loop. The premises
  are exactly the abstract parent's non-revert conditions plus the fuel
  bound, so abstract and Verity parents now share quantifier strength on the
  success arm. `verity_tx_composes_value_flow_and_rollback` stays as
  auxiliary regression evidence.
- Kill-lines on that same parent: `universalSuccessShapePredicate` factors
  the registered statement over the `Wiring`
  (`universal_parent_is_predicate_at_honest`); four wiring mutants refute it
  at the premise-satisfying witness `(10, 2, 3)`
  (`dropped_refund_leg_kill_line_refutes_universal_parent`,
  `misrouted_vault_kill_line_refutes_universal_parent`,
  `corrupted_refund_kill_line_refutes_universal_parent`,
  `single_request_kill_line_refutes_universal_parent`); three
  premise-necessity kill-lines refute the premise-dropped projections on the
  honest wiring (`zero_value_kill_line_refutes_dropped_positivity`,
  `underfunded_kill_line_refutes_dropped_funding`,
  `fuel_exhaustion_kill_line_refutes_dropped_fuel_premise`). Wave 2 scope
  kill-lines remain as executable premise witnesses.

PR #227 LOCKFILE non-rendered-row parser repair (2026-08-30)
------------------------------------------------------------

- `check_verity_provenance.py` used a raw `re.MULTILINE` regex to find the
  Verity row in `proofs/LOCKFILE.md`. Because `.` does not match newlines in
  that mode the pattern still matched a row that was present only inside an
  HTML comment (`<!-- | Verity | `...` | -->`) or a fenced code block
  (`` ``` `` / `~~~` delimiters), where GitHub Markdown does not render it.
  The check would then pass even though no canonical Verity pin appeared in
  the rendered table.
- Fix: add `_strip_non_rendered` (strips HTML comments via `re.DOTALL` and
  fenced blocks via backreference-terminated `re.MULTILINE | re.DOTALL`
  substitution) and pass its output to the existing `re.findall` instead of
  the raw file text. No other logic changed.
- Regression: `test_verity_provenance.py` gains two new mutants that remove
  the active Verity row and re-insert it exclusively inside an HTML comment
  and inside a fenced code block; both must be rejected with diagnostic
  `proofs/LOCKFILE.md must contain exactly one Verity pin row`.
- `python3 scripts/check_verity_provenance.py` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_verity_provenance.py` exit 0 (all prior mutants plus
  the two new non-rendered-region mutants).
- `python3 scripts/check_proof_escapes.py` exit 0; inventory unchanged.
- `bash scripts/check_provenance_guards.sh` exit 0.
- Receipt subject: tracked tree excluding this receipt, recorded as
  `validated-tree`.

PR #227 P2: CommonMark inline code-span closer recognition (2026-08-30)
------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Bug (Codex P2, discussion_r3889585498): `_strip_non_rendered` in
  `check_verity_provenance.py` detected a multiline code-span closer only
  when it occupied an otherwise blank line (whole-line `re.match` anchor).
  CommonMark §6.1 permits closers to appear anywhere on a line embedded in
  surrounding text (e.g. ``suffix ``` end``).  An adversary could therefore
  craft a LOCKFILE.md where the Verity row is enclosed in a code span whose
  closer is embedded inline on the following line; the parser would miss the
  closer, reach EOF, restore the buffered row as rendered content (unmatched
  code span is literal per §6.1), and accept the non-rendered pin.
- Fix: replace the whole-line `re.match` with `re.search` for an exact-length
  backtick run anywhere on the line (``(?<!`)`{N}(?!`)``).  Text after the
  closer on the closer line is emitted as rendered content; text before the
  closer (code-span body) is discarded.  The unmatched-code-span fallback
  (restore buffer when EOF is reached without a closer) is preserved.
- Regression additions to `test_verity_provenance.py`:
  - Negative (rejection): 3-backtick code-span opener with backtick-info
    string, Verity row, then ``suffix ``` end`` (inline exact-length closer)
    — the row is code content and must be rejected.
  - Positive (acceptance): same opener, Verity row, then
    ``suffix ```` end`` (unequal 4-backtick run) — no closer found, opener
    is literal, enclosed row remains rendered, check must pass.
- All prior regression cases preserved (HTML comment, unclosed-to-EOF,
  fence variants, backtick-code-span suppressors).
- `python3 scripts/check_proof_escapes.py` exit 0; inventory unchanged.
- `python3 scripts/check_validation_receipt.py` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/check_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `PYTHONOPTIMIZE=1 python3 scripts/test_audit_metadata.py` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_proof_escapes.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_verity_provenance.py` and
  `bash scripts/check_provenance_guards.sh` require the resolved Verity
  checkout (`.lake/packages/verity`), which is not built in this environment;
  unit-level `_strip_non_rendered` tests for all prior and new inline-closer
  cases verified in isolation (exit 0).
- No production Lean files changed; the `sorry`/`admit`/new-axiom/
  `native_decide` scan is empty.

  `validated-tree`.

PR #227 P2: CommonMark §4.6 raw HTML block suppression (2026-08-30)
--------------------------------------------------------------------

- Bug (Codex P2, discussion_r3889642189): `_strip_non_rendered` stripped
  fenced code blocks and HTML comments (§4.6 Type 2) but left raw HTML block
  types 1, 3–6 in place.  An adversary could place the Verity row inside e.g.
  ``<script>\n| Verity | `…` |\n</script>`` — GitHub Markdown does not render
  the table row inside a raw HTML block, but the regex still matched it, so
  the provenance check would incorrectly succeed.
- Fix: add CommonMark §4.6 HTML block detection to `_strip_non_rendered`.
  Module-level compiled patterns detect the opener of each type (Types 1, 3–6)
  and suppress subsequent lines until the type-specific end condition is met:
  - Type 1 (``<script|pre|style|textarea>``): closes on first line containing
    the corresponding ``</tag>`` pattern.
  - Type 3 (``<?``): closes on first line containing ``?>``.
  - Type 4 (``<!UPPERCASE``): closes on first line containing ``>``.
  - Type 5 (``<![CDATA[``): closes on first line containing ``]]>``.
  - Type 6 (block-level open/close tags): blank-line-terminated; the blank
    line itself is not part of the block.
  If the opener line also satisfies the end condition, the block closes
  immediately (one-line block) without entering the multi-line state.  Type 2
  (``<!-- … -->``) remains handled by `_strip_html_comments`, which already
  correctly skips code-span content.
- Regression additions to `test_verity_provenance.py`:
  - Negative (Type 1): ``<script>\n{verity_row}</script>`` — row inside script
    block → rejected.
  - Positive: ``<script>…</script>\n{verity_row}`` — row after closed script
    block is rendered → accepted.
  - Negative (Type 6): ``<div>\n{verity_row}`` (no blank line, extends to
    EOF) → rejected.
  - Positive: ``<div>\ncontent\n\n{verity_row}`` — row after blank-line-closed
    div block is rendered → accepted.
- `python3 scripts/check_proof_escapes.py` exit 0; inventory unchanged.
- `python3 scripts/check_validation_receipt.py` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/check_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `PYTHONOPTIMIZE=1 python3 scripts/test_audit_metadata.py` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_proof_escapes.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_verity_provenance.py` and
  `bash scripts/check_provenance_guards.sh` require the resolved Verity
  checkout (`.lake/packages/verity`), not built in this environment; unit-level
  `_strip_non_rendered` tests for all prior and new HTML-block cases verified
  in isolation (13 cases, all pass).
- No production Lean files changed; the `sorry`/`admit`/new-axiom/
  `native_decide` scan is empty.
- Receipt subject: tracked tree excluding this receipt, recorded as
  `validated-tree`.

PR #227 P2 batch: multiline code spans, Type 7 HTML blocks, whitespace
blank lines, same-line fence code-span closer (2026-08-30)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------

- Bugs (Codex P2, four discussions):

  discussion_r3889768393 — `_strip_html_comments` appended the entire backtick
  code-span (opener + content + closer) unchanged even when the span's interior
  crossed a newline.  An adversary could embed the Verity row inside a
  2-backtick (or higher) multiline inline code span:
  `` ``\n| Verity | `...` |\n`` ``.  GitHub renders the interior as inline
  code, not a table row, so the row is not visible; yet the regex matched it
  inside the old appended span.
  Fix: in `_strip_html_comments`, when the interior text between the opener's
  trailing backtick position and the closer's leading backtick position contains
  `\n`, suppress the entire span (don't append to result).

  discussion_r3889768395 — CommonMark §4.6 Type 7 HTML blocks (an open or
  close tag on a line by itself, blank-line-terminated) were not detected.  An
  adversary could use a custom element (`<custom-element>`) to suppress a
  following Verity row.
  Fix: add `_HTML7` pattern (`^ {0,3}</?[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9-]*…[ \t]*$`)
  and detect it in `_strip_non_rendered`'s HTML-block branch, with
  `html_block_end = None` (blank-line-terminated, same as Type 6).

  discussion_r3889768399 — The blank-line termination check for Types 6/7
  used `if not stripped:` (truly empty string), which missed lines containing
  only spaces or tabs.  CommonMark defines a blank line as one containing only
  spaces and tabs.
  Fix: change to `if not stripped.strip():`.

  discussion_r3889768402 — When an invalid fence opener's info string contains
  a backtick (making it a code-span opener rather than a fence opener), the
  code immediately entered span state even if the exact-length closer already
  appeared on the opener line itself.  Lines following the opener were buffered
  and either hidden (if a subsequent closer was found) or restored (if no
  closer before EOF).  The same-line-closer case should not enter span state
  at all — the span opens and closes on one line, making the line rendered
  content and the following lines (including the Verity row) also rendered.
  Fix: before entering code-span state, search for an exact-length closer in
  `m.group(2)` (the info string / rest of the opener line); if found, add
  the opener line to `out` and set `is_code_span = False`.

- Side effect of discussion_r3889768393 fix: the trailing backtick in info
  strings such as `` ```foo` `` forms a real CommonMark §6.1 inline code span
  in `_strip_html_comments` that covers `| Verity | ` in the restored buffer.
  Three tests previously marked positive (discussions r3889515392 and
  r3889585498 "unequal run" variants) are now correctly rejected: the Verity
  row in those inputs is not in a rendered table row.  Their test comments and
  expected values have been updated accordingly.

- Regression additions to `test_verity_provenance.py`:
  - Negative (r3889768393): `` ``\n{verity_row}``\n `` — row inside
    multiline 2-bt code span → rejected.
  - Positive: `` ``some\nspanning content``\n{verity_row} `` — row after a
    closed multiline span is rendered → accepted.
  - Negative (r3889768395): `<custom-element>\n{verity_row}` — row inside
    Type 7 block (extends to EOF) → rejected.
  - Positive: `<custom-element>\ncontent\n\n{verity_row}` — row after
    blank-line-closed Type 7 block → accepted.
  - Positive (r3889768399): `<div>\ncontent\n   \n{verity_row}` — whitespace-
    only blank line terminates Type 6 block → row after it accepted.
  - Positive: `<custom-element>\ncontent\n   \n{verity_row}` — same for
    Type 7 block.
  - Positive (r3889768402): `` ```foo```\n{verity_row}```\n `` — same-line
    closer on opener line; row after opener is rendered → accepted.
  - Negative: `` ```foo`\n{verity_row}```\n `` — no same-line closer;
    whole-line closer hides row → rejected.
  - Updated negative: three former positive "unequal-run" variants
    (discussions r3889515392, r3889585498) now correctly rejected due to
    info-backtick inline span covering `| Verity | `.

- `python3 scripts/check_proof_escapes.py` exit 0; inventory unchanged.
- `python3 scripts/check_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `PYTHONOPTIMIZE=1 python3 scripts/test_audit_metadata.py` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_proof_escapes.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_verity_provenance.py` and
  `bash scripts/check_provenance_guards.sh` require the resolved Verity
  checkout (`.lake/packages/verity`), not built in this environment;
  unit-level `_strip_non_rendered` / `_strip_html_comments` tests for all
  28 prior and new cases verified in isolation (all pass).

  `native_decide` scan is empty.

  `validated-tree`.

PR #227 P2: escaped backticks before HTML comments; quoted ">" in Type 7
attributes (2026-08-30)
------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Bugs (Codex P2, two discussions):

  discussion_r3889868433 — `_strip_html_comments` did not honour CommonMark §2.4
  backslash escapes for backtick characters.  The sequence `\`<!--\`` contains
  two backslash-escaped backticks (literals), not a code span.  But the loop
  treated the first `` ` `` as a code-span opener, matching the second `` ` ``
  as the closer with `<!--` as the (single-line, non-multiline) content.  The
  `<!--` was therefore never seen as an HTML comment opener; the Verity row
  between `\`<!--\`` and `-->` remained visible to the regex.
  Fix: before treating a `` ` `` as a code-span opener, count consecutive
  preceding backslashes; if the count is odd the backtick is escaped
  (CommonMark §2.4) and treated as a literal character, not a delimiter.

  discussion_r3889868441 — The `_HTML7` pattern used `[^<>]*` for the
  attribute portion of a Type 7 tag opener.  This rejected any `>` in the
  attribute text, even a `>` inside a quoted attribute value such as
  `title="a > b"`.  CommonMark §4.6 explicitly allows quoted values to contain
  `>`; an attacker could use `<custom-element title="a > b">` as an opener
  that the old pattern did not recognise, leaving the Verity row visible.
  Fix: replace `[^<>]*` with the full CommonMark §4.6 attribute grammar:
  zero-or-more attribute tokens each of the form `name` or `name=value` where
  value is double-quoted (`"[^"]*"`), single-quoted (`'[^']*'`), or unquoted
  (`[^"'=<>` `` ` `` `\x00-\x1f\s]+`).

- Regression additions to `test_verity_provenance.py`:
  - Negative (r3889868433): `\`<!--\`\n{verity_row}-->\n` — escaped backticks
    do not form a code span; `<!--...-->` is an HTML comment and the Verity row
    inside it is stripped → rejected.
  - Positive: `\\``<!--``\n{verity_row}` — even number of backslashes (escaped
    backslash + unescaped backtick) → code span still forms, `<!--` suppressed,
    Verity row after code span remains visible → accepted.
  - Negative (r3889868441): `<custom-element title="a > b">\n{verity_row}` —
    quoted `>` in attribute is now recognised as a valid Type 7 opener; row
    inside block → rejected.
  - Positive: `<custom-element title="a > b">\ncontent\n\n{verity_row}` — row
    after blank-line-terminated Type 7 block with quoted `>` → accepted.

- `python3 scripts/check_proof_escapes.py` exit 0; inventory unchanged.
- `python3 scripts/check_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `PYTHONOPTIMIZE=1 python3 scripts/test_audit_metadata.py` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_proof_escapes.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_verity_provenance.py` and
  `bash scripts/check_provenance_guards.sh` require the resolved Verity
  checkout (`.lake/packages/verity`), not built in this environment;
  unit-level `_strip_non_rendered` / `_strip_html_comments` tests for all
  33 prior and new cases verified in isolation (all pass).

PR #227 P2: ASCII-only fence-close whitespace; multiline inline HTML tag
suppression (2026-08-30)
------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Bugs (Codex P2, two discussions):

  discussion_r3889901971 — The closing-fence regex used `\s*$` to allow trailing
  whitespace after the fence character run.  Python's `\s` matches any Unicode
  whitespace, including characters such as U+2003 EM SPACE.  CommonMark §4.5
  explicitly restricts the trailing portion to "spaces only" (ASCII U+0020); any
  other whitespace character means the line is not a valid closer and the fence
  remains open.  An attacker could write ```` ``` ```` + EM SPACE so the checker
  mistakenly closed the fence, exposing a Verity row placed on the following line
  (which CommonMark still treats as inside the open fence).
  Fix: replace `\s*$` with `[ ]*$` in the fence-closer regex; only ASCII spaces
  are allowed as trailing whitespace on a closing fence line.

  discussion_r3889901979 — `_strip_html_comments` did not handle inline HTML open
  tags (CommonMark §6.6) that span multiple lines via quoted attribute values.
  An attacker could write `<span title="\n| Verity | … |\n">` so that the Verity
  row is the content of the quoted attribute value, not rendered table content.
  `_strip_non_rendered` only removes block-level HTML; the inline tag was left
  intact, and `_strip_html_comments` passed the attribute content through to the
  regex unchanged.
  Fix: add a new branch in `_strip_html_comments` for `<[A-Za-z]` / `</[A-Za-z]`
  that scans through the tag character by character.  When a quoted attribute value
  (`"…"` or `'…'`) contains `\n`, its interior is suppressed (replaced by the
  empty string between the two quote characters) so that a Verity row inside it
  cannot be seen by the caller's regex.  Single-line attribute values and tag
  content outside quotes are preserved unchanged.

- Regression additions to `test_verity_provenance.py`:
  - Negative (r3889901971): ```` ``` ````\nsome content\n```` ``` ````+EM SPACE\n{verity_row}
    — Unicode whitespace does not close the fence; Verity row suppressed → rejected.
  - Positive: ```` ``` ````\nsome content\n```` ``` ````+three ASCII spaces\n{verity_row}
    — ASCII trailing spaces are valid; fence closed, Verity row rendered → accepted.
  - Negative (r3889901979): `<span title="\n{verity_row}">\n`
    — Verity row inside multiline quoted attribute is raw HTML → rejected.
  - Positive: `<span title="foo">\n{verity_row}`
    — Verity row after a single-line inline HTML tag is rendered → accepted.

- `python3 scripts/check_proof_escapes.py` exit 0; inventory unchanged.
- `python3 scripts/check_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `PYTHONOPTIMIZE=1 python3 scripts/test_audit_metadata.py` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_proof_escapes.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_verity_provenance.py` and
  `bash scripts/check_provenance_guards.sh` require the resolved Verity
  checkout (`.lake/packages/verity`), not built in this environment;
  unit-level `_strip_non_rendered` / `_strip_html_comments` tests for all
  37 prior and new cases verified in isolation (all pass).
- No production Lean files changed; the `sorry`/`admit`/new-axiom/
  `native_decide` scan is empty.

PR #227 P2: abort inline-tag scan on invalid char; honour §2.4 escape before
`<!--` (2026-08-30)
------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Bugs (Codex P2, two discussions):

  discussion_r3889941662 — The inline HTML tag scanner accepted any `<` followed
  by a letter or `/` as the start of a tag and consumed characters until the next
  `>`.  This meant that `<x <!--…-->` was consumed entirely as a "tag" ending at
  the `>` of `-->`, without ever entering the HTML-comment branch.  The Verity row
  between `<!--` and `-->` was therefore added to `result` unchanged and was
  exposed to the caller's regex.
  Fix: inside the inline-tag inner loop, add an `elif text[j] == "<":` branch that
  sets `valid = False` and breaks.  If `valid` is False after the loop, only the
  initial `<` is appended to `result` and `i` is advanced by one; the rest of the
  text (including the `<!--` and everything after) is re-scanned from the character
  immediately following that `<`, so the comment branch correctly handles `<!--`.

  discussion_r3889941663 — The `elif text[i:i+4] == "<!--":` branch had no check
  for preceding backslash escapes.  Per CommonMark §2.4, an odd number of
  backslashes before `<` makes `<` a literal character; `\<!--` is therefore
  rendered as the literal text `<!--`, not an HTML comment.  Without the check,
  the function treated `\<!--` as an unclosed comment opener and discarded
  everything through EOF, causing false-positive rejection of any valid lockfile
  whose prose contained the sequence `\<!--` before the real Verity row.
  Fix: mirror the escaped-backtick logic — count consecutive preceding backslashes
  before entering the comment branch; if the count is odd, emit `<` literally and
  advance past it without entering comment-stripping mode.

- Regression additions to `test_verity_provenance.py`:
  - Negative (r3889941662): `<x <!--\n{verity_row}-->\n` — invalid inline tag
    (< in attribute position) aborts scanner; `<!--` is a real comment; row inside
    stripped → rejected.
  - Positive: `<x>\n{verity_row}` — valid inline tag (no invalid chars); Verity
    row after tag is rendered → accepted.
  - Positive (r3889941663): `\<!--\n{verity_row}` — odd-backslash-escaped `<`
    makes `<!--` literal text, not a comment opener; Verity row visible → accepted.
  - Negative: `\\<!--\n{verity_row}-->\n` — even number of backslashes (escaped
    backslash + unescaped `<`); `<!--` is a real comment; row inside stripped →
    rejected.

- `python3 scripts/check_proof_escapes.py` exit 0; inventory unchanged.
- `python3 scripts/check_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `PYTHONOPTIMIZE=1 python3 scripts/test_audit_metadata.py` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_proof_escapes.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_verity_provenance.py` and
  `bash scripts/check_provenance_guards.sh` require the resolved Verity
  checkout (`.lake/packages/verity`), not built in this environment;
  unit-level `_strip_non_rendered` / `_strip_html_comments` tests for all
  41 prior and new cases verified in isolation (all pass).
- No production Lean files changed; the `sorry`/`admit`/new-axiom/
  `native_decide` scan is empty.

  `validated-tree`.

PR #227 P2: preserve line boundaries when stripping HTML comments and code spans;
confine lockfile row regex to single physical line (2026-08-30)
------------------------------------------------------------------------

- Bug (Codex P2, discussion_r3889978820):

  `_strip_html_comments` removed HTML comments (and suppressed multiline code
  spans) by discarding the matched region entirely, including any embedded newlines.
  This caused content from two separate physical source lines to be concatenated
  in the output.  For example, a file containing:

      | Verity <!-- anything
      --> | `<pin>` |

  After stripping the comment `<!-- anything\n-->`, the function returned
  `| Verity  | `<pin>` |` — a single synthesized line that matches the Verity row
  regex, producing a false-positive acceptance despite the Verity label and pin
  living on different physical lines and therefore never forming a rendered table
  row.

  The same synthesis was possible with a suppressed multiline code span:

      | Verity ``anycontent
      `` | `<pin>` |

  Fix 1 — Line-boundary invariant: replace any stripped region (HTML comment or
  multiline code-span interior) with exactly as many `"\n"` characters as the
  region contained.  Single-line spans and comments produce no newlines and are
  unaffected.  Multiline regions now leave behind the newline skeleton, so content
  from different physical lines stays on different lines.

  Fix 2 — Confine lockfile row regex to a single physical line: change `\s*` to
  `[ \t]*` in the `re.findall` call that extracts Verity pins.  In `re.MULTILINE`
  mode, `\s*` matches `\n`, so even with the newline preserved in the stripped
  output the pattern could still bridge across it.  `[ \t]*` matches only ASCII
  space and horizontal tab, keeping each match within one physical line.

  Both fixes are necessary: Fix 1 alone cannot prevent synthesis if the regex
  can span newlines; Fix 2 alone cannot prevent synthesis if the stripping
  itself erases the newline separator.

- Regression additions to `test_verity_provenance.py`:
  - Negative (comment-synthesis, r3889978820): `| Verity <!-- anything\n--> |
    \`<pin>\` |\n` — multiline comment stripped with newline preserved; row
    regex confined to one line → rejected.
  - Positive (single-line comment): `| Verity <!-- note --> | \`<pin>\` |\n` —
    single-line comment stripped; synthesized row is the real row on one line →
    accepted.
  - Negative (span-synthesis, r3889978820): `| Verity ``anycontent\n`` |
    \`<pin>\` |\n` — multiline code span suppressed with newline preserved;
    row regex confined to one line → rejected.
  - Also corrected a Round 6 positive test whose input `<x>\n{verity_row}` was
    incorrect: `<x>` alone on its own line is a CommonMark Type 7 HTML block
    opener that suppresses the following row; corrected to `text <x> end\n{verity_row}`
    (inline tag in mixed-text context) which is rendered normally.

- `python3 scripts/check_proof_escapes.py` exit 0; inventory unchanged.
- `python3 scripts/check_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_public_claim_surfaces.py` exit 0.
- `PYTHONOPTIMIZE=1 python3 scripts/test_audit_metadata.py` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_proof_escapes.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `bash scripts/test_check_no_python_evidence.sh` exit 0.
- `python3 scripts/test_verity_provenance.py` and
  `bash scripts/check_provenance_guards.sh` require the resolved Verity
  checkout (`.lake/packages/verity`), not built in this environment;
  unit-level `_strip_non_rendered` / `_strip_html_comments` tests for all
  54 prior and new cases verified in isolation (all pass).
- No production Lean files changed; the `sorry`/`admit`/new-axiom/
  `native_decide` scan is empty.
- Receipt subject: tracked tree excluding this receipt, recorded as
  `validated-tree`.
